+# Kernel Parameters
+
+This section documents kernel command-line parameters supported by Asterinas.
+
+## Inherited from Linux
+
+### `init`
+
+Run the specified binary as `init`.
+
+Example:
+```text
+init=/bin/busybox
+```
+
+Notes:
+- The value is the path to the executable.
+- If omitted, Asterinas will fail to boot.
+
+### `console`
+
+Select console devices for kernel messages.
+This parameter may be specified multiple times.
+Kernel messages are delivered to each listed console.
+
+Valid values:
+- `tty0`
+- `ttyS0`
+- `hvc0`
+
+Examples:
+```text
+console=ttyS0
+console=ttyS0 console=hvc0
+```
+
+## Asterinas-specific
+
+### `ostd.log_level`
+
+Set the verbosity level for Asterinas's logs.
+
+Valid values:
+- `off`
+- `error`
+- `warn`
+- `info`
+- `debug`
+- `trace`
+
+Example:
+```text
+ostd.log_level=error
+```
+
+### `i8042.exist`
+
+Override ACPI's indication of whether a PS/2 (i8042) controller exists.
+
+Valid values:
+- `1`, `on`, `yes`, `true` or no value — treat the i8042 controller as present (force probing)
+- `0`, `off`, `no`, `false` - treat the i8042 controller as absent (skip probing)
+
+Examples:
+```text
+i8042.exist
+i8042.exist=1
+i8042.exist=0
+```

---
name: aster-code-review
description: Review code against Asterinas's persona-keyed coding guidelines and write a Markdown review file. Use when asked to review a Git change (diff mode) or a set of target files (files mode) for defects, or inside a write-test-review loop.
---

# aster-code-review

Review code against Asterinas's coding guidelines
— the persona-keyed pages under `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/`
— and write one Markdown review file.
The review is **recall-first**: miss as few real defects as possible.
It makes both objective calls (undeniable bugs) and subjective calls (guideline violations, each grounded in a cited rule).

There are two review modes, both **anchored at the current checkout (HEAD)**:

- **`diff <base>`** — a Git change:
  the **commit series** the branch adds over its merge-base with `<base>`
  — each commit's message *and* its diff,
  so the review covers per-commit intent and commit hygiene, not just the net change.
  (Commit first; uncommitted edits are not reviewed in diff mode.)
- **`files <path[:lines] ...>`**
  — target code: the working-tree contents of the named files,
  optionally narrowed to line ranges.

To review at a specific commit, check it out first (it becomes HEAD).

This skill is agent-agnostic (Claude Code and Codex).
Only step 3's spawn primitive differs between agents;
everything else is identical.

## Interface

However the skill is triggered,
it receives ONE raw argument string that begins with a mode word:

```
diff   <base>              <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
files  <path[:lines] ...>  <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
```

- `<base>` (diff) — **required**,
  any ref or SHA;
  reviews the commit series `merge-base(<base>, HEAD)..HEAD` (each commit's message + diff).
- `<path[:lines] ...>` (files) — **required**, one or more targets in the working tree.
  A target is a path, optionally `path:N-M` or `path:N-M,K-L` (1-based, inclusive);
  repeat a path to add ranges.
  Wrap a path in double quotes to allow spaces or colons (`"a: b.rs":10-20`).
- `<output>` — **required, last positional** (`cp src... dest` style);
  refuse to overwrite unless `--overwrite`.
- `--overwrite` — replace the output file if it already exists.
- `--per-persona-context` — `yes`: fan out one isolated agent per persona (best recall).
  `no`: one combined agent reviews all personas (cheaper, lower recall).
  `auto` (default) currently resolves to `yes`;
  it will become a benchmark-driven heuristic.
  Controls step 3 only.

**Do not pre-split the argument string yourself.**
Pass it verbatim, as a single quoted argument,
to `resolve_target.sh` (step 1)
— the script self-tokenizes (whitespace-split except inside double quotes),
which keeps the parse deterministic and identical across agents.

## Pipeline

Run these steps in order.
Steps 1 and 5 are deterministic scripts;
steps 6–8 use the model.

The helper scripts live in the skill's own `scripts/` directory,
at `.agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/` inside the repository under review.
**Invoke every script by its absolute path**
— the working directory is the code under review (the repo root), *not* the skill directory,
so a bare `scripts/…` is not found (notably on Codex, which runs commands from the repo root; do not `cd` into the skill dir either — `resolve_target.sh` needs the repo as its cwd).
Shells do not persist between commands,
so **set `SKILL` at the start of each command that uses it** (or inline the `$(git rev-parse …)`):

```sh
SKILL="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.agents/skills/aster-code-review"; "$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'
```

The steps below write `$SKILL/scripts/…` as shorthand for that absolute path.

1. **Resolve the target.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'` prints the canonical review input
   — the commit series (each commit's message + diff) in `diff` mode,
   annotated file excerpts in `files` mode;
   save it to a temp file.
   Run it again with `--meta` first (`"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" --meta '<raw args>'`) to get
   `mode=`, `base=`/`files=`, `head=`, `branch=`, `output=`, `overwrite=`, `per_persona_context=`;
   add `date=` (today) and an optional `title=` to make the meta file.
   Pass the raw argument string as a single quoted argument
   so the script's tokenizer sees it intact.
2. **Activate personas.**
   Pick which of the five personas run,
   from the reviewed paths — changed paths in `diff` mode,
   named paths in `files` mode (see *Activation*).
3. **Fan out.**
   Spawn the persona passes (see *Spawning*).
   With `per_persona_context` = `yes` or `auto` (the default),
   run **one isolated PASS per activated persona** — best recall.
   With `no`, run **one combined PASS** over all activated personas in a single context
   — cheaper, lower recall.
   Build each pass's prompt deterministically with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
   — one persona for fan-out, all activated personas for combined
   — and spawn the sub-agent with **that exact text**.
   Each pass returns a JSON array of comments;
   file each under its persona's `<fragdir>/<persona>.json` (the comment's `persona` field says which),
   so step 5 is unchanged.
4. **Collect** the per-persona JSON fragments.
5. **Assemble.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/assemble_review.sh" [--overwrite] <meta> <fragdir> <output>` performs the deterministic merge (group by persona in fixed order, sort by file→line, drop exact duplicates *within a persona*, write frontmatter, leave a `<!-- SUMMARY -->` placeholder).
   Pass `--overwrite` only if the user gave it;
   otherwise the script refuses to clobber an existing `<output>`.
6. **Verify** (see *Verification*).
7. **Consolidate** (see *Consolidation*).
8. **Summary.**
   Replace `<!-- SUMMARY -->` with a constructive summary over the final comments:
   what the code does well, the top issues ranked by severity,
   any structural recommendations.

## Activation

A persona runs unless the reviewed paths *provably* contain nothing in its remit (reviewed paths = changed paths in `diff` mode, named paths in `files` mode):

- **maintainability, development, security** — any reviewed code.
- **hardware** — a reviewed path is assembly (`*.S`, `*.asm`),
  an architecture directory (`.../arch/...`), or contains `asm!` / `global_asm!`.
- **documentation** — a reviewed path is `book/`, any `*.md`,
  syscall-coverage files (`*.scml`), or a user-facing API surface (a syscall or kernel parameter).

Activation is path-based and deterministic;
do not use a model to triage (a wrongly-skipped persona is a silent recall hole).

## Pass contract

The shared reviewer contract
— the review methodology and the JSON comment schema every pass must follow
— lives in [`scripts/pass_contract.md`](scripts/pass_contract.md),
inlined as the **stable head** of every pass prompt by `build_pass_prompt.sh` (step 3).
Do not paraphrase it into the prompt yourself:
retyping it breaks the byte-identical prefix the prompt cache relies on (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).
A pass reads only the persona block(s) it is given (selective exposure),
reviews the **REVIEW INPUT** at the foot of the prompt,
and returns a JSON array of comments per that schema.

## Spawning a pass

Build every pass prompt with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
and spawn the sub-agent with its **exact** output
— only a script keeps the prompt's stable head (contract + guideline) byte-identical across passes and reviews,
which is what lets the prompt cache reuse it (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).

**Default (`per_persona_context` = `yes`/`auto`)**
— one pass per activated persona,
each in a CLEAN context with only its own persona block (selective exposure):

- **Claude Code** — spawn a Task sub-agent per persona.
- **Codex** — run `codex exec "<build_pass_prompt.sh output>"` per persona
  (pass the built PROMPT TEXT as the argument).

**Never spawn a pass by re-running `aster_code_review.sh` / `run_agent.sh`,
nor by re-issuing the skill's own arguments** (e.g. `codex exec … diff <base> <out>`).
A pass is a *reviewer* invocation whose input is the `build_pass_prompt.sh` text
— not another run of this skill.
Re-entering the launcher spawns another orchestrator that spawns another … , an infinite fork bomb;
the launcher now refuses it (`ACR_AGENT_RUNNING`).

Passes are independent;
order does not matter.
Collect each pass's JSON.

**Combined (`per_persona_context` = `no`)**
— build ONE prompt with all activated personas (`"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input> <p1> <p2> ...`)
and run a single pass;
it reviews through every lens at once and tags each comment with its `persona`.
The input and spawn overhead are paid once,
so it is cheaper
— but a larger shared context dilutes focus,
so it finds fewer defects.
The default stays fan-out:
recall comes first,
and `no` is an explicit, opt-in concession.

## Verification (step 6)

For each comment, isolate the key premise it rests on
— especially an external-system fact (Linux/POSIX behaviour, the System V ABI, Rust semantics).
Try to **refute** it:
re-read the cited code and consult an authoritative source.
Assign a verdict:

- **confirmed** — keep the comment unchanged.
- **uncertain** — keep it, but prefix `problem` with `(unverified) `.
- **refuted** — remove the comment,
  and append it to a `## Retracted by verification` list at the foot of the file with a one-line reason.

Remove **only** on confident refutation;
an unsure check is `uncertain`, not `refuted`.
This is the only step that may remove a comment, and only false positives.

## Consolidation (step 7)

Find clusters of comments that share one root cause or one fix
(e.g. several manual lock/unlock pairs that all want RAII).
For each cluster, write a single unified fix
and repoint each member's `**Fix.**` at it ("Shared with the other `raii` comments: …").
**Never remove a comment**
— every symptom stays at its own location.

## Output format

The assembled file is:
frontmatter (`date`, `mode`, `base` or `files`, `head`, `branch`, optional `title`),
a `# Summary`,
then a `## <section>` per persona in the order **Maintainability, Correctness, Security, Hardware, Documentation** (Correctness is the Development persona's section).
Each comment is a `### \`file\` line N` subsection:
a quoted ` ```diff ` snippet,
then a prose line `` `<grounding>` (<severity>): <problem> ``,
then a `**Fix.**` paragraph.
Do not restructure what the script emits;
only fill the summary and apply the verify/consolidate edits.

     4	//!
     5	//! The format of the Asterinas command line string conforms

     7	//!
     8	//! <https://docs.kernel.org/admin-guide/kernel-parameters.html>

    14

    16	    collections::BTreeMap,

    18	    string::{String, ToString},
    19	    vec::Vec,
    20	};

    32	/// Defines a **single-value** `key=value` kernel command-line parameter.
    33	///
    34	/// During command-line parsing, all occurrences of `$name` are grouped and then
    35	/// handled with **last-wins** semantics.
    36	///
    37	/// # Arguments
    38	/// - `$name`: Parameter name (e.g. `"log_level"`).
    39	/// - `$storage`: Storage location for the parsed value. Its type must implement
    40	///   [`ParamStorage`] (e.g. [`spin::Once<T>`], [`AtomicU8`], [`AtomicU32`], [`AtomicBool`]).
    41	///
    42	/// # Parsing
    43	/// The stored value type `S::Value` must implement [`ParseParamValue`].
    44	/// By default, any type implementing [`core::str::FromStr`] automatically
    45	/// implements [`ParseParamValue`].
    46	///
    47	/// # Example
    48	/// ```ignore
    49	/// static LOG_LEVEL: AtomicU8 = AtomicU8::new(0);
    50	/// define_kv_param!("log_level", LOG_LEVEL);
    51	/// ```
    52	#[macro_export]
    53	macro_rules! define_kv_param {
    54	    ($name:expr, $storage:expr) => {
    55	        $crate::__define_param!(@late, $name, $storage, $crate::setup_kv_param);
    56	    };
    57	}
    58	
    59	/// Defines an **early** `key=value` kernel command-line parameter.
    60	///
    61	/// Almost same as [`define_kv_param!`], but it is processed earlier in the boot sequence.
    62	#[macro_export]
    63	macro_rules! define_kv_param_early {
    64	    ($name:expr, $storage:expr) => {
    65	        $crate::__define_param!(@early, $name, $storage, $crate::setup_kv_param);
    66	    };
    67	}
    68	
    69	/// Defines a **repeatable** `key=value` kernel command-line parameter.
    70	///
    71	/// Unlike [`define_kv_param!`], this parameter may appear multiple times; the
    72	/// framework collects **all** values and passes them to the parser in one shot.
    73	///
    74	/// # Arguments
    75	/// - `$name`: Parameter name (e.g. `"console"`).
    76	/// - `$storage`: Storage location for the parsed value. Its type must implement
    77	///   [`ParamStorage`].
    78	///
    79	/// # Parsing
    80	/// The stored value type `S::Value` must implement [`ParseRepeatableParamValue`].
    81	/// This crate provides a default implementation for `Vec<T>` where `T: FromStr`.
    82	///
    83	/// # Example
    84	/// ```ignore
    85	/// static CONSOLES: Once<Vec<String>> = Once::new();
    86	/// define_repeatable_kv_param!("console", CONSOLES);
    87	/// ```
    88	#[macro_export]
    89	macro_rules! define_repeatable_kv_param {
    90	    ($name:expr, $storage:expr) => {
    91	        $crate::__define_param!(@late, $name, $storage, $crate::setup_repeatable_kv_param);
    92	    };
    93	}
    94	
    95	/// Defines an **early** `key=value` kernel command-line parameter.
    96	///
    97	/// Almost same as [`define_repeatable_kv_param!`], but it is processed earlier in the boot sequence.
    98	#[macro_export]
    99	macro_rules! define_repeatable_kv_param_early {
   100	    ($name:expr, $storage:expr) => {
   101	        $crate::__define_param!(@early, $name, $storage, $crate::setup_repeatable_kv_param);
   102	    };
   103	}
   104	
   105	/// Defines a **flag** kernel command-line parameter.
   106	///
   107	/// A flag may appear as a bare token (e.g. `debug`) or with an optional value
   108	/// (e.g. `debug=1`). If the flag appears multiple times, the framework uses
   109	/// **last-wins** semantics.
   110	///
   111	/// # Arguments
   112	/// - `$name`: Flag name (e.g. `"debug"`).
   113	/// - `$storage`: Storage location for the parsed value. Its type must implement
   114	///   [`ParamStorage`].
   115	///
   116	/// # Parsing
   117	/// The stored value type `S::Value` must implement [`ParseFlag`]. This crate
   118	/// provides a default `bool` implementation:
   119	/// - `flag` / `flag=1` / `flag=on|yes|true` => `true`
   120	/// - `flag=0` / `flag=off|no|false` => `false`
   121	///
   122	/// # Example
   123	/// ```ignore
   124	/// static DEBUG: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);
   125	/// define_flag_param!("debug", DEBUG);
   126	/// ```
   127	#[macro_export]
   128	macro_rules! define_flag_param {
   129	    ($name:expr, $storage:expr) => {
   130	        $crate::__define_param!(@late, $name, $storage, $crate::setup_flag_param);
   131	    };
   132	}
   133	
   134	/// Defines an **early** `key=value` kernel command-line parameter.
   135	///
   136	/// Almost same as [`define_flag_param!`], but it is processed earlier in the boot sequence.
   137	#[macro_export]
   138	macro_rules! define_flag_param_early {
   139	    ($name:expr, $storage:expr) => {
   140	        $crate::__define_param!(@early, $name, $storage, $crate::setup_flag_param);
   141	    };
   142	}
   143	
   144	/// Helper for defining kernel command-line parameter.
   145	#[macro_export]
   146	macro_rules! __define_param {
   147	    (@late, $name:expr, $storage:expr, $setup:path) => {
   148	        const _: () = {
   149	            fn __kparam_setup(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)]) {
   150	                $setup(&$storage, occurrences, $name);
   151	            }
   152	            $crate::submit! {
   153	                $crate::KernelParam { name: $name, setup: __kparam_setup, early: false }
   154	            }
   155	        };
   156	    };
   157	
   158	    (@early, $name:expr, $storage:expr, $setup:path) => {
   159	        const _: () = {
   160	            fn __kparam_setup(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)]) {
   161	                $setup(&$storage, occurrences, $name);
   162	            }
   163	            $crate::submit! {
   164	                $crate::KernelParam { name: $name, setup: __kparam_setup, early: true }
   165	            }
   166	        };
   167	    };
   168	}
   169	
   170	/// The arguments passed to the init process, extracted from the kernel command line.
   171	#[derive(PartialEq, Debug)]
   172	pub struct InitprocArgs {
   173	    argv: Vec<CString>,
   174	    envp: Vec<CString>,
   175	}
   176	
   177	impl InitprocArgs {
   178	    /// Gets the argument vector (`argv`) of the init process.
   179	    pub fn argv(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
   180	        &self.argv
   181	    }
   182	
   183	    /// Gets the environment vector (`envp`) of the init process.
   184	    pub fn envp(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
   185	        &self.envp
   186	    }
   187	}
   188	
   189	/// Trait for types that can store a parsed parameter value.
   190	pub trait ParamStorage: Sync + 'static {
   191	    type Value;
   192	    fn store_param(&self, value: Self::Value);
   193	}
   194	
   195	impl<T: Send + Sync + 'static> ParamStorage for Once<T> {
   196	    type Value = T;
   197	    fn store_param(&self, value: T) {
   198	        self.call_once(|| value);
   199	    }
   200	}
   201	
   202	impl ParamStorage for AtomicU8 {
   203	    type Value = u8;
   204	    fn store_param(&self, value: u8) {
   205	        self.store(value, Ordering::Relaxed);
   206	    }
   207	}
   208	
   209	impl ParamStorage for AtomicU32 {
   210	    type Value = u32;
   211	    fn store_param(&self, value: u32) {
   212	        self.store(value, Ordering::Relaxed);
   213	    }
   214	}
   215	
   216	impl ParamStorage for AtomicBool {
   217	    type Value = bool;
   218	    fn store_param(&self, value: bool) {
   219	        self.store(value, Ordering::Relaxed);
   220	    }
   221	}
   222	
   223	/// Errors while parsing kernel command line parameters.
   224	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
   225	pub enum ParamError {
   226	    InvalidValue,
   227	}
   228	
   229	/// Parse a single-value key-value parameter (e.g., log_level=3).
   230	///
   231	/// If the parameter appears multiple times, the framework applies last-wins
   232	/// semantics (matching Linux behavior) and passes only the final value.
   233	pub trait ParseParamValue: Sized {
   234	    fn parse_param(value: &str) -> Result<Self, ParamError>;
   235	}
   236	
   237	/// Parse a repeatable key-value parameter (e.g., "console=ttyS0 console=ttyS1").
   238	///
   239	/// The framework collects all occurrences and passes the full slice.
   240	pub trait ParseRepeatableParamValue: Sized {
   241	    fn parse_all(values: &[&str]) -> Result<Self, ParamError>;
   242	}
   243	
   244	/// Parse a flag parameter (e.g., "ro", "debug", "nokaslr").
   245	///
   246	/// Flags may optionally accept a value (e.g., "debug=1" in Linux).
   247	pub trait ParseFlag: Sized {
   248	    /// Called when the flag is present on the command line.
   249	    /// `value` is `Some(v)` if written as "flag=v", `None` if bare.
   250	    fn parse_flag(value: Option<&str>) -> Result<Self, ParamError>;
   251	}
   252	
   253	/// Any `FromStr` type can be a single-value parameter.
   254	impl<T: FromStr> ParseParamValue for T {
   255	    fn parse_param(value: &str) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   256	        value.parse().map_err(|_| ParamError::InvalidValue)
   257	    }
   258	}
   259	
   260	/// A `Vec<T>` where `T: FromStr` can be a repeatable parameter.
   261	/// (No coherence conflict: `Vec<T>` does not implement `FromStr`.)
   262	impl<T: FromStr> ParseRepeatableParamValue for Vec<T> {
   263	    fn parse_all(values: &[&str]) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   264	        values
   265	            .iter()
   266	            .map(|v| v.parse().map_err(|_| ParamError::InvalidValue))
   267	            .collect()
   268	    }
   269	}
   270	
   271	/// `bool` as a flag: bare flag means `true`; "flag=1"/"flag=on" also accepted.
   272	impl ParseFlag for bool {
   273	    fn parse_flag(value: Option<&str>) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   274	        match value {
   275	            None | Some("1") | Some("on") | Some("yes") | Some("true") => Ok(true),
   276	            Some("0") | Some("off") | Some("no") | Some("false") => Ok(false),
   277	            _ => Err(ParamError::InvalidValue),
   278	        }
   279	    }
   280	}
   281	
   282	#[derive(Debug)]
   283	pub struct KernelParam {
   284	    pub name: &'static str,
   285	    pub setup: fn(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)]),
   286	    pub early: bool,
   287	}
   288	
   289	inventory::collect!(KernelParam);
   290	
   291	/// Helper for single-value key-value parameters (last-wins).
   292	pub fn setup_kv_param<S: ParamStorage>(
   293	    storage: &S,
   294	    occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)],
   295	    name: &str,
   296	) where
   297	    S::Value: ParseParamValue,
   298	{
   299	    let last = match occurrences.last() {
   300	        Some((_, val)) => *val, // Option<&str> is Copy
   301	        None => return,
   302	    };
   303	    match last {
   304	        Some(value) => match S::Value::parse_param(value) {
   305	            Ok(v) => storage.store_param(v),
   306	            Err(_) => log::warn!("invalid value for kernel parameter '{}'", name),
   307	        },
   308	        None => log::warn!("kernel parameter '{}' requires a value", name),
   309	    }
   310	}
   311	
   312	/// Helper for repeatable key-value parameters (all occurrences).
   313	pub fn setup_repeatable_kv_param<S: ParamStorage>(
   314	    storage: &S,
   315	    occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)],
   316	    name: &str,
   317	) where
   318	    S::Value: ParseRepeatableParamValue,
   319	{
   320	    let values: Vec<&str> = occurrences.iter().filter_map(|(_, val)| *val).collect();
   321	    if values.is_empty() {
   322	        log::warn!("repeatable parameter '{}' requires values", name);
   323	        return;
   324	    }
   325	    match S::Value::parse_all(&values) {
   326	        Ok(v) => storage.store_param(v),
   327	        Err(_) => log::warn!("invalid value for kernel parameter '{}'", name),
   328	    }
   329	}
   330	
   331	/// Helper for flag parameters (last-wins, optional value).
   332	pub fn setup_flag_param<S: ParamStorage>(
   333	    storage: &S,
   334	    occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)],
   335	    name: &str,
   336	) where
   337	    S::Value: ParseFlag,
   338	{
   339	    let last = match occurrences.last() {
   340	        Some((_, val)) => *val,
   341	        None => return,
   342	    };
   343	    match S::Value::parse_flag(last) {
   344	        Ok(v) => storage.store_param(v),
   345	        Err(_) => log::warn!("invalid value for flag '{}'", name),
   346	    }
   347	}
   348	
   349	pub static INIT_PROC_ARGS: Once<InitprocArgs> = Once::new();
   350	
   351	#[init_component]
   352	fn init() -> Result<(), ComponentInitError> {
   353	    INIT_PROC_ARGS.call_once(|| dispatch_params(ostd::boot::boot_info().kernel_cmdline.as_str()));
   354	
   355	    Ok(())
   356	}
   357	
   358	// Splits the command line string by spaces but preserve
   359	// ones that are protected by double quotes(`"`).
   360	fn split_arg(input: &str) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
   361	    let mut inside_quotes = false;
   362	
   363	    input.split(move |c: char| {
   364	        if c == '"' {
   365	            inside_quotes = !inside_quotes;
   366	        }
   367	
   368	        !inside_quotes && c.is_whitespace()
   369	    })
   370	}
   371	
   372	fn dispatch_params(cmdline: &str) -> InitprocArgs {
   373	    let mut result: InitprocArgs = InitprocArgs {
   374	        argv: Vec::new(),
   375	        envp: Vec::new(),
   376	    };
   377	
   378	    let mut kcmdline_end = false;
   379	
   380	    // Step 1: Tokenize and group by normalized parameter name.
   381	    let mut grouped: BTreeMap<String, Vec<(&str, Option<&str>)>> = BTreeMap::new();
   382	
   383	    for arg in split_arg(cmdline) {
   384	        // Everything after "--" goes to init.
   385	        if kcmdline_end {
   386	            result.argv.push(CString::new(arg).unwrap());
   387	            continue;
   388	        }
   389	        if arg == "--" {
   390	            kcmdline_end = true;
   391	            continue;
   392	        }
   393	
   394	        let (key, value) = match arg.find('=') {
   395	            Some(pos) => (&arg[..pos], Some(&arg[pos + 1..])),
   396	            None => (arg, None),
   397	        };
   398	        // Normalize hyphens to underscores (Linux compatibility)
   399	        let normalized = key.replace('-', "_");
   400	        // Group by normalized name, but keep the original key and value for forwarding.
   401	        grouped.entry(normalized).or_default().push((key, value));
   402	    }
   403	
   404	    // Step 2: Build lookup from registered param name to handler.
   405	    let registry: BTreeMap<&str, &KernelParam> = inventory::iter::<KernelParam>
   406	        .into_iter()
   407	        .map(|p| (p.name, p))
   408	        .collect();
   409	
   410	    // Step 3: Dispatch each group to its handler.
   411	    let mut recognized = Vec::new();
   412	    for (name, occurrences) in &grouped {
   413	        if let Some(param) = registry.get(name.as_str()) {
   414	            recognized.push((*param, occurrences));
   415	        } else {
   416	            // Unknown parameter: forward to init
   417	            if name.contains('.') {
   418	                // The entry contains a dot, which is treated as a module argument.
   419	                // Unrecognized module arguments are ignored.
   420	                continue;
   421	            } else if let Some((key, Some(value))) = occurrences.last().copied() {
   422	                // If the entry is not recognized, it is passed to the init process.
   423	                // Pattern 'entry=value' is treated as the init environment.
   424	                let envp_entry = CString::new(key.to_string() + "=" + value).unwrap();
   425	                result.envp.push(envp_entry);
   426	            } else if let Some((key, None)) = occurrences.last().copied() {
   427	                // If the entry is not recognized, it is passed to the init process.
   428	                // Pattern 'entry' without value is treated as the init argument.
   429	                let argv_entry = CString::new(key.to_string()).unwrap();
   430	                result.argv.push(argv_entry);
   431	            } else {
   432	                // This case should not happen since `occurrences` should never be empty.
   433	                debug_assert!(!occurrences.is_empty());
   434	            }
   435	        }
   436	    }
   437	
   438	    // Step 4: Call the setup function for each recognized parameter.
   439	    let (early_params, params): (Vec<_>, Vec<_>) =
   440	        recognized.into_iter().partition(|(p, _)| p.early);
   441	    early_params
   442	        .into_iter()
   443	        .chain(params)
   444	        .for_each(|(param, occurrences)| (param.setup)(occurrences));
   445	
   446	    result
   447	}
   448

   450	    ($($name:expr),+ $(,)?) => {
   451	        $(
   452	            const _: () = {

   454	                    $crate::setup_unimplemented(occurrences, $name);
   455	                }
   456	                $crate::submit! {
   457	                    $crate::KernelParam { name: $name, setup: __kparam_setup, early: false }
   458	                }
   459	            };
   460	        )+

   464	fn setup_unimplemented(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)], name: &str) {
   465	    if !occurrences.is_empty() {
   466	        log::warn!("kernel parameter '{}' is not yet implemented", name);

     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	//! Common parameter value types for the cmdline framework.
     4	//!
     5	//! This module provides Linux-style parsers that are frequently used by kernel
     6	//! command lines so users of this framework don't need to rewrite them.
     7	
     8	#![allow(dead_code)]
     9	
    10	extern crate alloc;
    11	
    12	use alloc::vec::Vec;
    13	
    14	use crate::{ParamError, ParseParamValue};
    15	
    16	/// Linux-style CPU list.
    17	///
    18	/// Examples:
    19	/// - `"1"`
    20	/// - `"1,2,10-20"`
    21	/// - `"100-2000:2/25"` (range with stride and optional group size)
    22	///
    23	/// The stored representation is a list of segments; expansion is optional.
    24	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    25	pub struct CpuList {
    26	    segments: Vec<CpuListSegment>,
    27	}
    28	
    29	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    30	pub struct CpuListSegment {
    31	    pub start: u32,
    32	    pub end: u32,
    33	    /// Step within range, default 1.
    34	    pub stride: u32,
    35	    /// Optional group size (the `/N` part). When present, stride selection is
    36	    /// applied within each group window.
    37	    pub group: Option<u32>,
    38	}
    39	
    40	/// Linux-style metric-suffixed u64 value.
    41	///
    42	/// Supports binary multiples (KiB-style):
    43	/// - `K` = 1024
    44	/// - `M` = 1024^2
    45	/// - `G` = 1024^3
    46	/// - `T` = 1024^4
    47	/// - `P` = 1024^5
    48	///
    49	/// Case-insensitive.
    50	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    51	pub struct MetricU64(pub u64);
    52	
    53	impl CpuList {
    54	    pub fn segments(&self) -> &[CpuListSegment] {
    55	        &self.segments
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    pub fn contains(&self, cpu: u32) -> bool {
    59	        self.segments.iter().any(|s| segment_contains(s, cpu))
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    /// Expand to concrete CPU IDs, returning at most `max_elems` elements.
    63	    ///
    64	    /// Note: if the CPU list expands to more than `max_elems`, the result is truncated.
    65	    pub fn expand_bounded(&self, max_elems: usize) -> Result<Vec<u32>, ParamError> {
    66	        if max_elems == 0 {
    67	            return Ok(Vec::new());
    68	        }
    69	
    70	        let mut out = Vec::new();
    71	
    72	        // Helper: push a cpu id; if we reached the bound, stop expansion.
    73	        let push_or_done = |out: &mut Vec<u32>, cpu: u32| -> bool {
    74	            if out.len() >= max_elems {
    75	                return true;
    76	            }
    77	            out.push(cpu);
    78	            out.len() >= max_elems
    79	        };
    80	
    81	        for s in &self.segments {
    82	            if s.stride == 0 {
    83	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
    84	            }
    85	            if let Some(g) = s.group
    86	                && g == 0
    87	            {
    88	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
    89	            }
    90	            if s.start > s.end {
    91	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
    92	            }
    93	
    94	            // Fast path: no group => arithmetic progression from start to end by stride.
    95	            if s.group.is_none() {
    96	                let mut cur = s.start;
    97	                while cur <= s.end {
    98	                    if push_or_done(&mut out, cur) {
    99	                        return Ok(out);
   100	                    }
   101	
   102	                    match cur.checked_add(s.stride) {
   103	                        Some(n) => cur = n,
   104	                        None => break,
   105	                    }
   106	                }
   107	                continue;
   108	            }
   109	
   110	            // Grouped selection:
   111	            // for each group window [start+gb, start+gb+g), emit start+gb+within for within=0,stride,2*stride...
   112	            let g = s.group.unwrap();
   113	            let stride = s.stride;
   114	
   115	            let mut gb: u32 = 0;
   116	            while let Some(v) = s.start.checked_add(gb) {
   117	                let group_start = v;
   118	                if group_start > s.end {
   119	                    break;
   120	                }
   121	
   122	                let mut within: u32 = 0;
   123	                while within < g {
   124	                    let cpu = match group_start.checked_add(within) {
   125	                        Some(v) => v,
   126	                        None => break,
   127	                    };
   128	                    if cpu > s.end {
   129	                        break;
   130	                    }
   131	
   132	                    if push_or_done(&mut out, cpu) {
   133	                        return Ok(out);
   134	                    }
   135	
   136	                    match within.checked_add(stride) {
   137	                        Some(n) => within = n,
   138	                        None => break,
   139	                    }
   140	                }
   141	
   142	                match gb.checked_add(g) {
   143	                    Some(n) => gb = n,
   144	                    None => break,
   145	                }
   146	            }
   147	        }
   148	
   149	        Ok(out)
   150	    }
   151	}
   152	
   153	fn segment_contains(s: &CpuListSegment, cpu: u32) -> bool {
   154	    if cpu < s.start || cpu > s.end {
   155	        return false;
   156	    }
   157	    if s.stride == 0 {
   158	        return false;
   159	    }
   160	
   161	    let offset = cpu - s.start;
   162	    if let Some(g) = s.group {
   163	        if g == 0 {
   164	            return false;
   165	        }
   166	        let in_group_offset = offset % g;
   167	        in_group_offset.is_multiple_of(s.stride)
   168	    } else {
   169	        offset.is_multiple_of(s.stride)
   170	    }
   171	}
   172	
   173	impl ParseParamValue for CpuList {
   174	    fn parse_param(value: &str) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   175	        if value.is_empty() {
   176	            return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   177	        }
   178	
   179	        let mut segments = Vec::new();
   180	        for part in value.split(',') {
   181	            if part.is_empty() {
   182	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   183	            }
   184	            segments.push(parse_cpu_segment(part)?);
   185	        }
   186	
   187	        Ok(CpuList { segments })
   188	    }
   189	}
   190	
   191	fn parse_cpu_segment(s: &str) -> Result<CpuListSegment, ParamError> {
   192	    // Grammar (pragmatic):
   193	    //   <range> [":" <stride> ["/" <group>] ]
   194	    // where <range> := <n> | <n> "-" <m>
   195	    let (range_part, tail) = match s.split_once(':') {
   196	        Some((a, b)) => (a, Some(b)),
   197	        None => (s, None),
   198	    };
   199	
   200	    let (start, end) = if let Some((a, b)) = range_part.split_once('-') {
   201	        (parse_u32(a)?, parse_u32(b)?)
   202	    } else {
   203	        let n = parse_u32(range_part)?;
   204	        (n, n)
   205	    };
   206	
   207	    if start > end {
   208	        return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   209	    }
   210	
   211	    let (stride, group) = match tail {
   212	        None => (1u32, None),
   213	        Some(t) => {
   214	            if t.is_empty() {
   215	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   216	            }
   217	            if let Some((stride_s, group_s)) = t.split_once('/') {
   218	                let stride = parse_u32(stride_s)?;
   219	                let group = parse_u32(group_s)?;
   220	                (stride, Some(group))
   221	            } else {
   222	                (parse_u32(t)?, None)
   223	            }
   224	        }
   225	    };
   226	
   227	    if stride == 0 {
   228	        return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   229	    }
   230	
   231	    if let Some(g) = group
   232	        && g == 0
   233	    {
   234	        return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   235	    }
   236	
   237	    Ok(CpuListSegment {
   238	        start,
   239	        end,
   240	        stride,
   241	        group,
   242	    })
   243	}
   244	
   245	fn parse_u32(s: &str) -> Result<u32, ParamError> {
   246	    if s.is_empty() {
   247	        return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   248	    }
   249	    s.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| ParamError::InvalidValue)
   250	}
   251	
   252	impl ParseParamValue for MetricU64 {
   253	    fn parse_param(value: &str) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   254	        if value.is_empty() {
   255	            return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   256	        }
   257	
   258	        let (num_part, suf) = match value.chars().last() {
   259	            Some(c) if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() => (&value[..value.len() - c.len_utf8()], Some(c)),
   260	            _ => (value, None),
   261	        };
   262	
   263	        if num_part.is_empty() {
   264	            return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   265	        }
   266	
   267	        let base: u64 = num_part.parse().map_err(|_| ParamError::InvalidValue)?;
   268	        let mul: u64 = match suf.map(|c| c.to_ascii_uppercase()) {
   269	            None => 1,
   270	            Some('K') => 1024u64,
   271	            Some('M') => 1024u64.pow(2),
   272	            Some('G') => 1024u64.pow(3),
   273	            Some('T') => 1024u64.pow(4),
   274	            Some('P') => 1024u64.pow(5),
   275	            _ => return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue),
   276	        };
   277	
   278	        base.checked_mul(mul)
   279	            .map(MetricU64)
   280	            .ok_or(ParamError::InvalidValue)
   281	    }
   282	}

   387	    fn cpu_list_expand_bounded() {
   388	        let cl = CpuList::parse_param("10-20:2").unwrap(); // 10,12,14,16,18,20 => 6 elems
   389	        let v = cl.expand_bounded(6).unwrap();
   390	        assert_eq!(v.as_slice(), &[10u32, 12u32, 14u32, 16u32, 18u32, 20u32]);

   394	    }

   396	    #[ktest]
   397	    fn cpu_list_expand_bounded_large_range_stride_fast() {
   398	        // 0-4_000_000_000:1024 should not scan the whole range; it should just step by 1024.
   399	        // We only ask for a few elements to ensure it returns quickly and correctly.
   400	        let cl = CpuList::parse_param("0-4000000000:1024").unwrap();
   401	        let v = cl.expand_bounded(4).unwrap();
   402	        assert_eq!(v.as_slice(), &[0u32, 1024u32, 2048u32, 3072u32]);
   403	    }

   405	    #[ktest]
   406	    fn cpu_list_expand_bounded_group_large_range_fast() {
   407	        // Grouped selection still shouldn't scan by +1.
   408	        // 0-4_000_000_000:2/4 => in each group of 4 select offsets 0 and 2.
   409	        // First few selected: 0,2,4,6,8,10,...
   410	        let cl = CpuList::parse_param("0-4000000000:2/4").unwrap();
   411	        let v = cl.expand_bounded(6).unwrap();
   412	        assert_eq!(v.as_slice(), &[0u32, 2u32, 4u32, 6u32, 8u32, 10u32]);
   413	    }

   417	        // Ensure the last (partial) group is clipped by end.
   418	        // 0-5:2/4 => groups [0..3] => 0,2; [4..7] => 4,6 but end=5 so only 4.
   419	        let cl = CpuList::parse_param("0-5:2/4").unwrap();

   175	    /// Checks if the kernel command line contains the "i8042.exist" option.

   179	
   180	    fn read_configuration(&mut self) -> Result<Configuration, I8042ControllerError> {
   181	        self.wait_and_send_command(Command::ReadConfiguration)?;
   182	        self.wait_and_recv_data()
   183	            .map(Configuration::from_bits_retain)
   184	    }
   185	
   186	    fn write_configuration(&mut self, config: &Configuration) -> Result<(), I8042ControllerError> {
   187	        self.wait_and_send_command(Command::WriteConfiguration)?;
   188	        self.wait_and_send_data(config.bits())
   189	    }
   190	
   191	    fn wait_and_send_command(&mut self, command: Command) -> Result<(), I8042ControllerError> {
   192	        spin_wait_until(Timeout::Short, || self.send_command(command).ok())
   193	            .ok_or(I8042ControllerError::OutputBusy)
   194	    }
   195	
   196	    fn send_command(&mut self, command: Command) -> Result<(), I8042ControllerError> {
   197	        if !self.read_status().contains(Status::INPUT_BUFFER_IS_FULL) {
   198	            self.write_command(command as u8);
   199	            Ok(())
   200	        } else {
   201	            Err(I8042ControllerError::OutputBusy)
   202	        }
   203	    }
   204	
   205	    fn read_status(&self) -> Status {
   206	        Status::from_bits_retain(self.status_or_command_port.read())
   207	    }
   208	
   209	    fn write_command(&mut self, command: u8) {
   210	        self.status_or_command_port.write(command);
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    pub(super) fn wait_and_send_data(&mut self, data: u8) -> Result<(), I8042ControllerError> {
   214	        spin_wait_until(Timeout::Short, || self.send_data(data).ok())
   215	            .ok_or(I8042ControllerError::OutputBusy)
   216	    }
   217	
   218	    pub(super) fn write_to_second_port(&mut self, data: u8) -> Result<(), I8042ControllerError> {
   219	        self.wait_and_send_command(Command::WriteToSecondPort)?;
   220	        self.wait_and_send_data(data)
   221	    }
   222	
   223	    pub(super) fn send_data(&mut self, data: u8) -> Result<(), I8042ControllerError> {
   224	        if !self.read_status().contains(Status::INPUT_BUFFER_IS_FULL) {
   225	            self.write_data(data);
   226	            Ok(())
   227	        } else {
   228	            Err(I8042ControllerError::OutputBusy)
   229	        }
   230	    }
   231	
   232	    fn write_data(&mut self, data: u8) {
   233	        self.data_port.write(data);
   234	    }
   235	
   236	    pub(super) fn wait_and_recv_data(&mut self) -> Result<u8, I8042ControllerError> {
   237	        spin_wait_until(Timeout::Short, || self.receive_data()).ok_or(I8042ControllerError::NoInput)
   238	    }
   239	
   240	    /// Waits a long time for the data to be received.
   241	    ///
   242	    /// This is usually used when performing a reset that takes a long time to complete.
   243	    pub(super) fn wait_long_and_recv_data(&mut self) -> Result<u8, I8042ControllerError> {
   244	        spin_wait_until(Timeout::Long, || self.receive_data()).ok_or(I8042ControllerError::NoInput)
   245	    }
   246	
   247	    /// Waits for the specified data to be received.
   248	    ///
   249	    /// This is typically used when waiting for an acknowledgment of a command. Any garbage data
   250	    /// before the acknowledgment is ignored.
   251	    pub(super) fn wait_for_specific_data(
   252	        &mut self,
   253	        data: &[u8],
   254	    ) -> Result<u8, I8042ControllerError> {
   255	        spin_wait_until(Timeout::Short, || {
   256	            self.receive_data().filter(|val| data.contains(val))
   257	        })
   258	        .ok_or(I8042ControllerError::NoInput)
   259	    }
   260	
   261	    pub(super) fn receive_data(&mut self) -> Option<u8> {
   262	        if self.read_status().contains(Status::OUTPUT_BUFFER_IS_FULL) {
   263	            Some(self.read_data())
   264	        } else {
   265	            None
   266	        }
   267	    }
   268	
   269	    fn read_data(&self) -> u8 {
   270	        self.data_port.read()
   271	    }
   272	
   273	    fn flush_output_buffer(&mut self) {
   274	        while self.receive_data().is_some() {}
   275	    }
   276	}
   277	
   278	/// Timeout in milliseconds for sending commands or receiving data.
   279	///
   280	/// Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.17.9/source/drivers/input/serio/libps2.c#L344>
   281	#[repr(u16)]
   282	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
   283	enum Timeout {
   284	    /// Short timeout for normal commands (500 ms).
   285	    Short = 500,
   286	    /// Long timeout for the reset command (4000 ms).
   287	    Long = 4000,
   288	}
   289	
   290	/// Spins and waits until the timeout occurs or `f` returns `Some(_)`.
   291	//
   292	// TODO: The timeout is relatively large, up to several seconds. Therefore, spinning here is not
   293	// appropriate. The code needs to be refactored to use asynchronous interrupts.
   294	fn spin_wait_until<F, R>(timeout: Timeout, mut f: F) -> Option<R>
   295	where
   296	    F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
   297	{
   298	    use ostd::arch::{read_tsc, tsc_freq};
   299	
   300	    const MSEC_PER_SEC: u64 = 1000;
   301	
   302	    if let Some(res) = f() {
   303	        return Some(res);
   304	    }
   305	
   306	    let current = read_tsc();
   307	    let distance = tsc_freq() / MSEC_PER_SEC * (timeout as u16 as u64);
   308	    loop {
   309	        if let Some(res) = f() {
   310	            return Some(res);
   311	        }
   312	        if read_tsc().wrapping_sub(current) >= distance {
   313	            return None;
   314	        }
   315	        core::hint::spin_loop();
   316	    }
   317	}
   318	
   319	/// Errors that can occur when initializing the i8042 controller.
   320	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
   321	pub(super) enum I8042ControllerError {
   322	    NotPresent,
   323	    ControllerTestFailed,
   324	    FirstPortTestFailed,
   325	    SecondPortTestFailed,
   326	    OutputBusy,
   327	    NoInput,
   328	    DeviceResetFailed,
   329	    DeviceUnknown,
   330	    DeviceAllocIrqFailed,
   331	}
   332	
   333	/// The commands that can be sent to the PS/2 controller.
   334	///
   335	/// Reference: <https://wiki.osdev.org/I8042_PS/2_Controller#PS/2_Controller_Commands>.
   336	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
   337	#[repr(u8)]
   338	enum Command {
   339	    ReadConfiguration = 0x20,
   340	    WriteConfiguration = 0x60,
   341	    DisableSecondPort = 0xA7,
   342	    EnableSecondPort = 0xA8,
   343	    TestSecondPort = 0xA9,
   344	    TestController = 0xAA,
   345	    TestFirstPort = 0xAB,
   346	    DisableFirstPort = 0xAD,
   347	    EnableFirstPort = 0xAE,
   348	    WriteToSecondPort = 0xD4,
   349	}
   350	
   351	bitflags! {
   352	    /// The configuration of the PS/2 controller.
   353	    ///
   354	    /// Reference: <https://wiki.osdev.org/I8042_PS/2_Controller#PS/2_Controller_Configuration_Byte>.
   355	    struct Configuration: u8 {
   356	        /// First PS/2 port interrupt (1 = enabled, 0 = disabled)
   357	        const FIRST_PORT_INTERRUPT_ENABLED = 1 << 0;
   358	        /// Second PS/2 port interrupt (1 = enabled, 0 = disabled, only if 2 PS/2 ports supported)
   359	        const SECOND_PORT_INTERRUPT_ENABLED = 1 << 1;
   360	        /// System Flag (1 = system passed POST, 0 = your OS shouldn't be running)
   361	        const SYSTEM_POST_PASSED = 1 << 2;
   362	        /// First PS/2 port clock (1 = disabled, 0 = enabled)
   363	        const FIRST_PORT_CLOCK_DISABLED = 1 << 4;
   364	        /// Second PS/2 port clock (1 = disabled, 0 = enabled, only if 2 PS/2 ports supported)
   365	        const SECOND_PORT_CLOCK_DISABLED = 1 << 5;
   366	        /// First PS/2 port translation (1 = enabled, 0 = disabled)
   367	        const FIRST_PORT_TRANSLATION_ENABLED = 1 << 6;
   368	    }
   369	}
   370	
   371	bitflags! {
   372	    /// The status of the i8042 PS/2 controller.
   373	    ///
   374	    /// Reference: <https://wiki.osdev.org/I8042_PS/2_Controller#Status_Register>.
   375	    struct Status: u8 {
   376	        /// Output buffer status (0 = empty, 1 = full)
   377	        /// Must be set before attempting to read data from port 0x60.
   378	        const OUTPUT_BUFFER_IS_FULL = 1 << 0;
   379	        /// Input buffer status (0 = empty, 1 = full)
   380	        /// Must be clear before attempting to write data to IO port 0x60 or IO port 0x64.
   381	        const INPUT_BUFFER_IS_FULL = 1 << 1;
   382	        /// System Flag
   383	        /// Meant to be cleared on reset and set by firmware (via. PS/2 Controller Configuration Byte)
   384	        /// if the system passes self tests (POST).
   385	        const SYSTEM_FLAG = 1 << 2;
   386	        /// Command or data (0 = data, 1 = command)
   387	        /// Data written to input buffer is data for PS/2 device or command for controller.
   388	        const IS_COMMAND = 1 << 3;
   389	        /// Time-out error (0 = no error, 1 = time-out error)
   390	        const TIME_OUT_ERROR = 1 << 6;
   391	        /// Parity error (0 = no error, 1 = parity error)
   392	        const PARITY_ERROR = 1 << 7;
   393	    }
   394	}

   397	aster_cmdline::define_flag_param!("i8042.exist", I8042_EXIST);
   130	
   131	//--------------------------------------------------------------------------
   132	// The first kernel thread
   133	//--------------------------------------------------------------------------
   134	
   135	// The main function of the first (non-idle) kernel thread
   136	fn first_kthread() {
   137	    println!("[kernel] Spawn the first kernel thread");
   138	
   139	    let init_mnt_ns = MountNamespace::get_init_singleton();
   140	    let fs_resolver = init_mnt_ns.new_path_resolver();
   141	    init_in_first_kthread(&fs_resolver);
   142	
   143	    print_banner();
   144	
   145	    INIT_PROCESS.call_once(|| {
   146	        let karg = INIT_PROC_ARGS.get().unwrap();
   147	        let init_path = INIT_PATH.get().expect("Init process path not specified.");
   148	        spawn_init_process(init_path, karg.argv().to_vec(), karg.envp().to_vec())
   149	            .expect("Run init process failed.")
   150	    });
   151	}
   152	
   153	static INIT_PROCESS: Once<Arc<Process>> = Once::new();
   154	
   155	fn init_in_first_kthread(path_resolver: &PathResolver) {
   156	    component::init_all(InitStage::Kthread, component::parse_metadata!()).unwrap();
   157	    // Work queue should be initialized before interrupt is enabled,
   158	    // in case any irq handler uses work queue as bottom half
   159	    crate::thread::work_queue::init_in_first_kthread();
   160	    crate::device::init_in_first_kthread();
   161	    crate::net::init_in_first_kthread();
   162	    crate::fs::init_in_first_kthread(path_resolver);
   163	    crate::ipc::init_in_first_kthread();
   164	    #[cfg(any(target_arch = "x86_64", target_arch = "riscv64"))]
   165	    crate::vdso::init_in_first_kthread();
   166	}
   167	
   168	fn print_banner() {
   169	    println!("");
   170	    println!("{}", logo_ascii_art::get_gradient_color_version());
   171	}
   172	
   173	pub(crate) fn on_first_process_startup(ctx: &Context) {
   174	    component::init_all(InitStage::Process, component::parse_metadata!()).unwrap();
   175	    crate::device::init_in_first_process(ctx).unwrap();
   176	    crate::fs::init_in_first_process(ctx);
   177	    crate::process::init_in_first_process(ctx);
   178	}

   181	aster_cmdline::define_kv_param!("init", INIT_PATH);
    95	pub struct SystemConsole {
    96	    inner: Arc<dyn Terminal>,
    97	}
    98	
    99	impl SystemConsole {
   100	    /// Returns the singleton instance of the console device.
   101	    pub fn singleton() -> &'static Arc<SystemConsole> {
   102	        static INSTANCE: Once<Arc<SystemConsole>> = Once::new();
   103	
   104	        INSTANCE.call_once(|| {
   105	            // TODO: Support specifying multiple TTY devices, e.g., "console=hvc0 console=tty0".
   106	            let console_name = CONSOLES
   107	                .get()
   108	                .and_then(|consoles| consoles.first().map(|s| s.as_str()))
   109	                .unwrap_or("tty0");
   110	
   111	            let device = match console_name {
   112	                "tty0" => Some(Arc::new(Tty0Device) as _),
   113	                "ttyS0" => serial0_device().cloned().map(|device| device as _),
   114	                "hvc0" => hvc0_device().cloned().map(|device| device as _),
   115	                _ => None,
   116	            };
   117	            let inner = device.unwrap_or_else(|| {
   118	                warn!(
   119	                    "'{}' console not found, falling back to 'tty0'",
   120	                    console_name
   121	                );
   122	                Arc::new(Tty0Device) as _
   123	            });
   124	
   125	            Arc::new(Self { inner })
   126	        })
   127	    }
   128	
   129	    /// Returns the terminal associated with the console device.
   130	    pub fn terminal(&self) -> &Arc<dyn Terminal> {
   131	        &self.inner
   132	    }
   133	}
   134	
   135	impl Device for SystemConsole {
   136	    fn type_(&self) -> DeviceType {
   137	        DeviceType::Char
   138	    }
   139	
   140	    fn id(&self) -> DeviceId {
   141	        DeviceId::new(MajorId::new(5), MinorId::new(1))
   142	    }
   143	
   144	    fn devtmpfs_path(&self) -> Option<String> {
   145	        Some("console".into())
   146	    }
   147	
   148	    fn open(&self) -> Result<Box<dyn FileIo>> {
   149	        self.inner.open()
   150	    }
   151	}
   152	
   153	pub(super) fn init_in_first_process() -> Result<()> {
   154	    char::register(Arc::new(Tty0Device))?;
   155	    char::register(Arc::new(TtyDevice))?;
   156	    char::register(SystemConsole::singleton().clone())?;
   157	
   158	    Ok(())
   159	}

   161	static CONSOLES: Once<Vec<String>> = Once::new();
   162	aster_cmdline::define_repeatable_kv_param!("console", CONSOLES);

codex
So far there’s no new `unsafe` in the kernel side, and the parsing helpers reject malformed numeric values rather than coercing them. I’m checking the source and construction of `kernel_cmdline` next, because any possible `CString::new(...).unwrap()` security finding depends on whether an interior NUL can reach this parser.

codex
The boot info stores the command line as a Rust `String` copied from early boot `&str`, so ordinary C-string termination likely strips a raw NUL before this layer. I’m doing one more pass over the architecture parsers and the new registry behavior to avoid reporting a boundary-validation issue that cannot actually be triggered here.

    46	fn parse_kernel_commandline(boot_params: &BootParams) -> Option<&str> {
    47	    if boot_params.ext_cmd_line_ptr != 0 {
    48	        // TODO: We can support the above 4GiB command line after setting up
    49	        // linear mappings. By far, we cannot log the error because the serial is
    50	        // not up. Proceed as if there was no command line.
    51	        return None;
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    if boot_params.hdr.cmd_line_ptr == 0 || boot_params.hdr.cmdline_size == 0 {
    55	        return None;
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    let cmdline_ptr = paddr_to_vaddr(boot_params.hdr.cmd_line_ptr as usize);
    59	    let cmdline_len = boot_params.hdr.cmdline_size as usize;
    60	    // SAFETY: The command line is safe to read because of the contract with the loader.
    61	    let cmdline = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(cmdline_ptr as *const u8, cmdline_len) };
    62	
    63	    // Now, unfortunately, there are silent errors because the serial is not up.
    64	    core::ffi::CStr::from_bytes_until_nul(cmdline)
    65	        .ok()?
    66	        .to_str()
    67	        .ok()
    68	}

    17	fn parse_bootloader_name(mb2_info: &BootInformation) -> Option<&'static str> {

   351	        if self.cur_ptr >= self.region_end {

   109	/// The entry point of the Rust code portion of Asterinas.
   110	///
   111	/// `BOOTSTRAP_HART_ID` is initialized to be `hart_id` and accessible after calling this.
   112	///
   113	/// # Safety
   114	///
   115	/// - This function must be called only once at a proper timing in the BSP's boot assembly code.
   116	/// - The caller must follow C calling conventions and put the right arguments in registers.
   117	// SAFETY: The name does not collide with other symbols.
   118	#[unsafe(no_mangle)]
   119	unsafe extern "C" fn riscv_boot(hart_id: usize, device_tree_paddr: usize) -> ! {
   120	    early_println!("Enter riscv_boot");
   121	
   122	    // We will only write it once. Other processors will only read it.
   123	    // SAFETY: We don't create Rust references, so there are no aliasing problems. Other processors
   124	    // have not been booted yet, so there are no data races.
   125	    unsafe { BOOTSTRAP_HART_ID = hart_id as u32 };
   126	
   127	    let device_tree_ptr = paddr_to_vaddr(device_tree_paddr) as *const u8;
   128	    let fdt = unsafe { fdt::Fdt::from_ptr(device_tree_ptr).unwrap() };
   129	    DEVICE_TREE.call_once(|| fdt);
   130	
   131	    use crate::boot::{EARLY_INFO, EarlyBootInfo, start_kernel};
   132	
   133	    EARLY_INFO.call_once(|| EarlyBootInfo {
   134	        bootloader_name: parse_bootloader_name(),
   135	        kernel_cmdline: parse_kernel_commandline(),
   136	        initramfs: parse_initramfs(),
   137	        acpi_arg: parse_acpi_arg(),
   138	        framebuffer_arg: parse_framebuffer_info(),
   139	        memory_regions: parse_memory_regions(),
   140	    });
   141	
   142	    // SAFETY: The safety is guaranteed by the safety preconditions and the fact that we call it
   143	    // once after setting up necessary resources.
   144	    unsafe { start_kernel() };
   145	}

    91	    // Now we only support the 48 bits PA width.
    92	    assert_eq!(palen, 48);
    93	    // Now we only support the 48 bits VA width.
    94	    assert_eq!(valen, 48);
    95	    // Now we require IOCSR support be present.
    96	    assert!(support_iocsr);
    97	}

    99	/// The entry point of the Rust code portion of Asterinas.
   100	///
   101	/// Reference: <https://docs.kernel.org/arch/loongarch/booting.html#information-passed-from-bootloader-to-kernel>
   102	///
   103	/// # Safety
   104	///
   105	/// - This function must be called only once at a proper timing in the BSP's boot assembly code.
   106	/// - The caller must follow C calling conventions and put the right arguments in registers.
   107	// SAFETY: The name does not collide with other symbols.
   108	#[unsafe(no_mangle)]
   109	unsafe extern "C" fn loongarch_boot(
   110	    _efi_boot: usize,
   111	    cmdline_paddr: usize,
   112	    systab_paddr: usize,
   113	) -> ! {
   114	    check_cpu_config();
   115	
   116	    let systab_ptr = paddr_to_vaddr(systab_paddr) as *const EfiSystemTable;
   117	    let systab = unsafe { &*(systab_ptr) };
   118	    EFI_SYSTEM_TABLE.call_once(|| systab);
   119	
   120	    let device_tree_ptr =
   121	        paddr_to_vaddr(systab.device_tree().expect("device tree not found")) as *const u8;
   122	    let fdt = unsafe { fdt::Fdt::from_ptr(device_tree_ptr).unwrap() };
   123	    DEVICE_TREE.call_once(|| fdt);
   124	
   125	    let cmdline_ptr = paddr_to_vaddr(cmdline_paddr) as *const i8;
   126	    let cmdline = unsafe { CStr::from_ptr(cmdline_ptr) }.to_str();
   127	
   128	    use crate::boot::{EARLY_INFO, EarlyBootInfo, start_kernel};

   140	    // once after setting up necessary resources.

    27	/// Creates and schedules the init process to run.
    28	pub fn spawn_init_process(
    29	    executable_path: &str,
    30	    argv: Vec<CString>,
    31	    envp: Vec<CString>,
    32	) -> Result<Arc<Process>> {
    33	    // Ensure the path for init process executable is absolute.
    34	    debug_assert!(executable_path.starts_with('/'));
    35	
    36	    let process = create_init_process(executable_path, argv, envp)?;
    37	
    38	    set_session_and_group(&process);
    39	
    40	    process.run();
    41	
    42	    Ok(process)
    43	}
    44	
    45	fn create_init_process(
    46	    executable_path: &str,
    47	    argv: Vec<CString>,
    48	    envp: Vec<CString>,
    49	) -> Result<Arc<Process>> {
    50	    let fs = {
    51	        let fs_resolver = MountNamespace::get_init_singleton().new_path_resolver();
    52	        ThreadFsInfo::new(fs_resolver)
    53	    };
    54	    let fs_path = FsPath::try_from(executable_path)?;
    55	    let elf_path = fs.resolver().read().lookup(&fs_path)?;
    56	
    57	    let pid = allocate_posix_tid();
    58	    let vmar = Vmar::new(ProcessVm::new(elf_path.clone()));
    59	    let resource_limits = new_resource_limits_for_init();
    60	    let nice = Nice::default();
    61	    let oom_score_adj = 0;
    62	    let sig_dispositions = Arc::new(Mutex::new(SigDispositions::default()));
    63	    let user_ns = UserNamespace::get_init_singleton().clone();
    64	
    65	    let init_proc = Process::new(
    66	        pid,
    67	        vmar,
    68	        resource_limits,
    69	        nice,
    70	        oom_score_adj,
    71	        sig_dispositions,
    72	        user_ns,
    73	    );
    74	
    75	    let init_task = create_init_task(pid, &init_proc, fs, elf_path, argv, envp)?;
    76	    init_proc.tasks().lock().insert(init_task).unwrap();
    77	
    78	    Ok(init_proc)
    79	}
    80	
    81	fn set_session_and_group(process: &Arc<Process>) {
    82	    // Locking order: session table -> group table -> process table -> process group
    83	    let mut session_table_mut = process_table::session_table_mut();
    84	    let mut group_table_mut = process_table::group_table_mut();
    85	    let mut process_table_mut = process_table::process_table_mut();
    86	
    87	    // Create a new process group and session for the process
    88	    process.set_new_session(
    89	        &mut process.process_group.lock(),
    90	        &mut session_table_mut,
    91	        &mut group_table_mut,
    92	    );
    93	
    94	    // Add the new process to the global table
    95	    process_table_mut.insert(process.pid(), process.clone());
    96	}
    97	
    98	/// Creates the init task from the given executable file.
    99	fn create_init_task(
   100	    tid: Tid,
   101	    process: &Arc<Process>,
   102	    fs: ThreadFsInfo,
   103	    elf_path: Path,
   104	    argv: Vec<CString>,
   105	    envp: Vec<CString>,
   106	) -> Result<Arc<Task>> {
   107	    let credentials = Credentials::new_root();
   108	
   109	    let (elf_load_info, elf_abs_path) = {
   110	        let path_resolver = fs.resolver().read();
   111	
   112	        let program_to_load =
   113	            ProgramToLoad::build_from_file(elf_path.clone(), &path_resolver, argv, envp)?;
   114	        let vmar = process.lock_vmar();
   115	        let elf_load_info = program_to_load.load_to_vmar(vmar.unwrap(), &path_resolver)?;
   116	        let elf_abs_path = path_resolver.make_abs_path(&elf_path).into_string();
   117	
   118	        (elf_load_info, elf_abs_path)
   119	    };
   120

     5	//! FIXME: It will print to all `virtio-console` devices, which is not a good choice.

    13	
    14	/// Prints the formatted arguments to the standard output.
    15	pub fn _print(args: fmt::Arguments) {
    16	    // We must call `all_devices_lock` instead of `all_devices` here, as `all_devices` invokes the
    17	    // `clone` method of `String` and `Arc`, which may lead to a deadlock when there is low memory
    18	    // in the heap. (The heap allocator will log a message when memory is low.)
    19	    //
    20	    // Also, holding the lock will prevent the logs from interleaving.
    21	    let devices = aster_console::all_devices_lock();
    22	
    23	    struct Printer<'a>(
    24	        SpinLockGuard<'a, BTreeMap<String, Arc<dyn AnyConsoleDevice>>, LocalIrqDisabled>,
    25	    );
    26	    impl Write for Printer<'_> {
    27	        fn write_str(&mut self, s: &str) -> fmt::Result {
    28	            if self.0.is_empty() {
    29	                ostd::early_print!("{}", s);
    30	            } else {
    31	                for console in self.0.values() {
    32	                    console.send(s.as_bytes());
    33	                }
    34	            }
    35	            Ok(())
    36	        }
    37	    }
    38	
    39	    Printer(devices).write_fmt(args).unwrap();
    40	}

     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	//! Logger injection.
     4	//!
     5	//! OSTD allows its client to inject a custom implementation of logger.
     6	//! If no such logger is injected,
     7	//! then OSTD falls back to a built-in logger that
     8	//! simply dumps all log records with [`crate::console::early_print`].
     9	//!
    10	//! OSTD's logger facility relies on the [log] crate.
    11	//! Both an OSTD client and OSTD itself use the macros from the `log` crate
    12	//! such as `error`, `info`, and `debug` to emit log records.
    13	//! The injected logger is required to implement the [`log::Log`] trait.
    14	//!
    15	//! [log]: https://docs.rs/log
    16	
    17	use core::str::FromStr;
    18	
    19	use log::{LevelFilter, Metadata, Record};
    20	use spin::Once;
    21	
    22	use crate::boot::EARLY_INFO;
    23	
    24	/// Injects a logger.
    25	///
    26	/// This method can be called at most once; calling it more than once has no effect.
    27	///
    28	/// # Requirements
    29	///
    30	/// As the logger may be invoked in stringent situations,
    31	/// such as an interrupt handler, an out-of-memory handler, or a panic handler,
    32	/// a logger should be implemented to be
    33	/// _short_ (simple and non-sleeping) and
    34	/// _heapless_ (not trigger heap allocations).
    35	/// Failing to do so may cause the kernel to panic or deadlock.
    36	pub fn inject_logger(new_logger: &'static dyn log::Log) {
    37	    LOGGER.backend.call_once(|| new_logger);
    38	}
    39	
    40	/// Initializes the logger. Users should avoid using the log macros before this function is called.
    41	pub(crate) fn init() {
    42	    let level = get_log_level().unwrap_or(LevelFilter::Off);
    43	    log::set_max_level(level);
    44	    log::set_logger(&LOGGER).unwrap();
    45	}
    46	
    47	static LOGGER: Logger = Logger::new();
    48	
    49	struct Logger {
    50	    backend: Once<&'static dyn log::Log>,
    51	}
    52	
    53	impl Logger {
    54	    const fn new() -> Self {
    55	        Self {
    56	            backend: Once::new(),
    57	        }
    58	    }
    59	}
    60	
    61	impl log::Log for Logger {
    62	    fn enabled(&self, metadata: &Metadata) -> bool {
    63	        if let Some(logger) = self.backend.get() {
    64	            return logger.enabled(metadata);
    65	        };
    66	
    67	        // Default implementation.
    68	        true
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    fn log(&self, record: &Record) {
    72	        if let Some(logger) = self.backend.get() {
    73	            return logger.log(record);
    74	        };
    75	
    76	        // Default implementation.
    77	        let level = record.level();
    78	        crate::console::early_print(format_args!("{}: {}\n", level, record.args()));
    79	    }
    80	
    81	    fn flush(&self) {
    82	        if let Some(logger) = self.backend.get() {
    83	            logger.flush();
    84	        };
    85	    }
    86	}
    87	
    88	fn get_log_level() -> Option<LevelFilter> {
    89	    let kcmdline = EARLY_INFO.get().unwrap().kernel_cmdline;
    90	
    91	    // Although OSTD is agnostic of the parsing of the kernel command line,
    92	    // the logger assumes that it follows the Linux kernel command line format.
    93	    // We search for the `ostd.log_level=ARGUMENT` pattern in string.
    94	    let value = kcmdline
    95	        .split(' ')
    96	        .find(|arg| arg.starts_with("ostd.log_level="))
    97	        .map(|arg| arg.split('=').next_back().unwrap_or_default())?;
    98	
    99	    LevelFilter::from_str(value).ok()
   100	}

---
name: aster-code-review
description: Review code against Asterinas's persona-keyed coding guidelines and write a Markdown review file. Use when asked to review a Git change (diff mode) or a set of target files (files mode) for defects, or inside a write-test-review loop.

# aster-code-review

Review code against Asterinas's coding guidelines
— the persona-keyed pages under `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/`
— and write one Markdown review file.
The review is **recall-first**: miss as few real defects as possible.
It makes both objective calls (undeniable bugs) and subjective calls (guideline violations, each grounded in a cited rule).

There are two review modes, both **anchored at the current checkout (HEAD)**:

- **`diff <base>`** — a Git change:
  the **commit series** the branch adds over its merge-base with `<base>`
  — each commit's message *and* its diff,
  so the review covers per-commit intent and commit hygiene, not just the net change.
  (Commit first; uncommitted edits are not reviewed in diff mode.)
- **`files <path[:lines] ...>`**
  — target code: the working-tree contents of the named files,
  optionally narrowed to line ranges.

To review at a specific commit, check it out first (it becomes HEAD).

This skill is agent-agnostic (Claude Code and Codex).
Only step 3's spawn primitive differs between agents;
everything else is identical.

## Interface

However the skill is triggered,
it receives ONE raw argument string that begins with a mode word:

```
diff   <base>              <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
files  <path[:lines] ...>  <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
```

- `<base>` (diff) — **required**,
  any ref or SHA;
  reviews the commit series `merge-base(<base>, HEAD)..HEAD` (each commit's message + diff).
- `<path[:lines] ...>` (files) — **required**, one or more targets in the working tree.
  A target is a path, optionally `path:N-M` or `path:N-M,K-L` (1-based, inclusive);
  repeat a path to add ranges.
  Wrap a path in double quotes to allow spaces or colons (`"a: b.rs":10-20`).
- `<output>` — **required, last positional** (`cp src... dest` style);
  refuse to overwrite unless `--overwrite`.
- `--overwrite` — replace the output file if it already exists.
- `--per-persona-context` — `yes`: fan out one isolated agent per persona (best recall).
  `no`: one combined agent reviews all personas (cheaper, lower recall).
  `auto` (default) currently resolves to `yes`;
  it will become a benchmark-driven heuristic.
  Controls step 3 only.

**Do not pre-split the argument string yourself.**
Pass it verbatim, as a single quoted argument,
to `resolve_target.sh` (step 1)
— the script self-tokenizes (whitespace-split except inside double quotes),
which keeps the parse deterministic and identical across agents.

## Pipeline

Run these steps in order.
Steps 1 and 5 are deterministic scripts;
steps 6–8 use the model.

The helper scripts live in the skill's own `scripts/` directory,
at `.agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/` inside the repository under review.
**Invoke every script by its absolute path**
— the working directory is the code under review (the repo root), *not* the skill directory,
so a bare `scripts/…` is not found (notably on Codex, which runs commands from the repo root; do not `cd` into the skill dir either — `resolve_target.sh` needs the repo as its cwd).
Shells do not persist between commands,
so **set `SKILL` at the start of each command that uses it** (or inline the `$(git rev-parse …)`):

```sh
SKILL="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.agents/skills/aster-code-review"; "$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'
```

The steps below write `$SKILL/scripts/…` as shorthand for that absolute path.

1. **Resolve the target.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'` prints the canonical review input
   — the commit series (each commit's message + diff) in `diff` mode,
   annotated file excerpts in `files` mode;
   save it to a temp file.
   Run it again with `--meta` first (`"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" --meta '<raw args>'`) to get
   `mode=`, `base=`/`files=`, `head=`, `branch=`, `output=`, `overwrite=`, `per_persona_context=`;
   add `date=` (today) and an optional `title=` to make the meta file.
   Pass the raw argument string as a single quoted argument
   so the script's tokenizer sees it intact.
2. **Activate personas.**
   Pick which of the five personas run,
   from the reviewed paths — changed paths in `diff` mode,
   named paths in `files` mode (see *Activation*).
3. **Fan out.**
   Spawn the persona passes (see *Spawning*).
   With `per_persona_context` = `yes` or `auto` (the default),
   run **one isolated PASS per activated persona** — best recall.
   With `no`, run **one combined PASS** over all activated personas in a single context
   — cheaper, lower recall.
   Build each pass's prompt deterministically with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
   — one persona for fan-out, all activated personas for combined
   — and spawn the sub-agent with **that exact text**.
   Each pass returns a JSON array of comments;
   file each under its persona's `<fragdir>/<persona>.json` (the comment's `persona` field says which),
   so step 5 is unchanged.
4. **Collect** the per-persona JSON fragments.
5. **Assemble.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/assemble_review.sh" [--overwrite] <meta> <fragdir> <output>` performs the deterministic merge (group by persona in fixed order, sort by file→line, drop exact duplicates *within a persona*, write frontmatter, leave a `<!-- SUMMARY -->` placeholder).
   Pass `--overwrite` only if the user gave it;
   otherwise the script refuses to clobber an existing `<output>`.
6. **Verify** (see *Verification*).
7. **Consolidate** (see *Consolidation*).
8. **Summary.**
   Replace `<!-- SUMMARY -->` with a constructive summary over the final comments:
   what the code does well, the top issues ranked by severity,
   any structural recommendations.

## Activation

A persona runs unless the reviewed paths *provably* contain nothing in its remit (reviewed paths = changed paths in `diff` mode, named paths in `files` mode):

- **maintainability, development, security** — any reviewed code.
- **hardware** — a reviewed path is assembly (`*.S`, `*.asm`),
  an architecture directory (`.../arch/...`), or contains `asm!` / `global_asm!`.
- **documentation** — a reviewed path is `book/`, any `*.md`,
  syscall-coverage files (`*.scml`), or a user-facing API surface (a syscall or kernel parameter).

Activation is path-based and deterministic;
do not use a model to triage (a wrongly-skipped persona is a silent recall hole).

## Pass contract

The shared reviewer contract
— the review methodology and the JSON comment schema every pass must follow
— lives in [`scripts/pass_contract.md`](scripts/pass_contract.md),
inlined as the **stable head** of every pass prompt by `build_pass_prompt.sh` (step 3).
Do not paraphrase it into the prompt yourself:
retyping it breaks the byte-identical prefix the prompt cache relies on (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).
A pass reads only the persona block(s) it is given (selective exposure),
reviews the **REVIEW INPUT** at the foot of the prompt,
and returns a JSON array of comments per that schema.

## Spawning a pass

Build every pass prompt with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
and spawn the sub-agent with its **exact** output
— only a script keeps the prompt's stable head (contract + guideline) byte-identical across passes and reviews,
which is what lets the prompt cache reuse it (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).

**Default (`per_persona_context` = `yes`/`auto`)**
— one pass per activated persona,
each in a CLEAN context with only its own persona block (selective exposure):

- **Claude Code** — spawn a Task sub-agent per persona.
- **Codex** — run `codex exec "<build_pass_prompt.sh output>"` per persona
  (pass the built PROMPT TEXT as the argument).

**Never spawn a pass by re-running `aster_code_review.sh` / `run_agent.sh`,
nor by re-issuing the skill's own arguments** (e.g. `codex exec … diff <base> <out>`).
A pass is a *reviewer* invocation whose input is the `build_pass_prompt.sh` text
— not another run of this skill.
Re-entering the launcher spawns another orchestrator that spawns another … , an infinite fork bomb;
the launcher now refuses it (`ACR_AGENT_RUNNING`).

Passes are independent;
order does not matter.
Collect each pass's JSON.

**Combined (`per_persona_context` = `no`)**
— build ONE prompt with all activated personas (`"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input> <p1> <p2> ...`)
and run a single pass;
it reviews through every lens at once and tags each comment with its `persona`.
The input and spawn overhead are paid once,
so it is cheaper
— but a larger shared context dilutes focus,
so it finds fewer defects.
The default stays fan-out:
recall comes first,
and `no` is an explicit, opt-in concession.

## Verification (step 6)

For each comment, isolate the key premise it rests on
— especially an external-system fact (Linux/POSIX behaviour, the System V ABI, Rust semantics).
Try to **refute** it:
re-read the cited code and consult an authoritative source.
Assign a verdict:

- **confirmed** — keep the comment unchanged.
- **uncertain** — keep it, but prefix `problem` with `(unverified) `.
- **refuted** — remove the comment,
  and append it to a `## Retracted by verification` list at the foot of the file with a one-line reason.

Remove **only** on confident refutation;
an unsure check is `uncertain`, not `refuted`.
This is the only step that may remove a comment, and only false positives.

## Consolidation (step 7)

Find clusters of comments that share one root cause or one fix
(e.g. several manual lock/unlock pairs that all want RAII).
For each cluster, write a single unified fix
and repoint each member's `**Fix.**` at it ("Shared with the other `raii` comments: …").
**Never remove a comment**
— every symptom stays at its own location.

## Output format

The assembled file is:
frontmatter (`date`, `mode`, `base` or `files`, `head`, `branch`, optional `title`),
a `# Summary`,
then a `## <section>` per persona in the order **Maintainability, Correctness, Security, Hardware, Documentation** (Correctness is the Development persona's section).
Each comment is a `### \`file\` line N` subsection:
a quoted ` ```diff ` snippet,
then a prose line `` `<grounding>` (<severity>): <problem> ``,
then a `**Fix.**` paragraph.
Do not restructure what the script emits;
only fill the summary and apply the verify/consolidate edits.
