    32	/// Defines a **single-value** `key=value` kernel command-line parameter.
    33	///
    34	/// During command-line parsing, all occurrences of `$name` are grouped and then
    35	/// handled with **last-wins** semantics.
    36	///
    37	/// # Arguments
    38	/// - `$name`: Parameter name (e.g. `"log_level"`).
    39	/// - `$storage`: Storage location for the parsed value. Its type must implement
    40	///   [`ParamStorage`] (e.g. [`spin::Once<T>`], [`AtomicU8`], [`AtomicU32`], [`AtomicBool`]).
    41	///
    42	/// # Parsing
    43	/// The stored value type `S::Value` must implement [`ParseParamValue`].
    44	/// By default, any type implementing [`core::str::FromStr`] automatically
    45	/// implements [`ParseParamValue`].
    46	///
    47	/// # Example
    48	/// ```ignore
    49	/// static LOG_LEVEL: AtomicU8 = AtomicU8::new(0);
    50	/// define_kv_param!("log_level", LOG_LEVEL);
    51	/// ```
    52	#[macro_export]
    53	macro_rules! define_kv_param {
    54	    ($name:expr, $storage:expr) => {
    55	        $crate::__define_param!(@late, $name, $storage, $crate::setup_kv_param);
    56	    };
    57	}

   109	/// **last-wins** semantics.
   110	///
   111	/// # Arguments

   120	/// - `flag=0` / `flag=off|no|false` => `false`

   122	/// # Example

   148	        const _: () = {

   189	/// Trait for types that can store a parsed parameter value.
   190	pub trait ParamStorage: Sync + 'static {
   191	    type Value;
   192	    fn store_param(&self, value: Self::Value);
   193	}
   194	
   195	impl<T: Send + Sync + 'static> ParamStorage for Once<T> {
   196	    type Value = T;
   197	    fn store_param(&self, value: T) {
   198	        self.call_once(|| value);
   199	    }
   200	}
   201	
   202	impl ParamStorage for AtomicU8 {
   203	    type Value = u8;
   204	    fn store_param(&self, value: u8) {
   205	        self.store(value, Ordering::Relaxed);
   206	    }
   207	}
   208	
   209	impl ParamStorage for AtomicU32 {
   210	    type Value = u32;
   211	    fn store_param(&self, value: u32) {
   212	        self.store(value, Ordering::Relaxed);
   213	    }
   214	}

   216	impl ParamStorage for AtomicBool {
   217	    type Value = bool;
   218	    fn store_param(&self, value: bool) {
   219	        self.store(value, Ordering::Relaxed);
   220	    }
   221	}
   222	
   223	/// Errors while parsing kernel command line parameters.
   224	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
   225	pub enum ParamError {
   226	    InvalidValue,
   227	}
   228	
   229	/// Parse a single-value key-value parameter (e.g., log_level=3).
   230	///
   231	/// If the parameter appears multiple times, the framework applies last-wins
   232	/// semantics (matching Linux behavior) and passes only the final value.
   233	pub trait ParseParamValue: Sized {
   234	    fn parse_param(value: &str) -> Result<Self, ParamError>;
   235	}
   236	
   237	/// Parse a repeatable key-value parameter (e.g., "console=ttyS0 console=ttyS1").
   238	///
   239	/// The framework collects all occurrences and passes the full slice.
   240	pub trait ParseRepeatableParamValue: Sized {
   241	    fn parse_all(values: &[&str]) -> Result<Self, ParamError>;
   242	}
   243	
   244	/// Parse a flag parameter (e.g., "ro", "debug", "nokaslr").
   245	///
   246	/// Flags may optionally accept a value (e.g., "debug=1" in Linux).
   247	pub trait ParseFlag: Sized {
   248	    /// Called when the flag is present on the command line.
   249	    /// `value` is `Some(v)` if written as "flag=v", `None` if bare.
   250	    fn parse_flag(value: Option<&str>) -> Result<Self, ParamError>;
   251	}
   252	
   253	/// Any `FromStr` type can be a single-value parameter.
   254	impl<T: FromStr> ParseParamValue for T {
   255	    fn parse_param(value: &str) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   256	        value.parse().map_err(|_| ParamError::InvalidValue)
   257	    }
   258	}
   259	
   260	/// A `Vec<T>` where `T: FromStr` can be a repeatable parameter.
   261	/// (No coherence conflict: `Vec<T>` does not implement `FromStr`.)
   262	impl<T: FromStr> ParseRepeatableParamValue for Vec<T> {
   263	    fn parse_all(values: &[&str]) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   264	        values
   265	            .iter()
   266	            .map(|v| v.parse().map_err(|_| ParamError::InvalidValue))
   267	            .collect()
   268	    }
   269	}
   270	
   271	/// `bool` as a flag: bare flag means `true`; "flag=1"/"flag=on" also accepted.
   272	impl ParseFlag for bool {
   273	    fn parse_flag(value: Option<&str>) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   274	        match value {
   275	            None | Some("1") | Some("on") | Some("yes") | Some("true") => Ok(true),
   276	            Some("0") | Some("off") | Some("no") | Some("false") => Ok(false),
   277	            _ => Err(ParamError::InvalidValue),
   278	        }
   279	    }
   280	}

   282	#[derive(Debug)]
   283	pub struct KernelParam {
   284	    pub name: &'static str,
   285	    pub setup: fn(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)]),
   286	    pub early: bool,
   287	}
   288	
   289	inventory::collect!(KernelParam);
   290	
   291	/// Helper for single-value key-value parameters (last-wins).
   292	pub fn setup_kv_param<S: ParamStorage>(
   293	    storage: &S,
   294	    occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)],
   295	    name: &str,
   296	) where
   297	    S::Value: ParseParamValue,
   298	{
   299	    let last = match occurrences.last() {
   300	        Some((_, val)) => *val, // Option<&str> is Copy
   301	        None => return,
   302	    };
   303	    match last {
   304	        Some(value) => match S::Value::parse_param(value) {
   305	            Ok(v) => storage.store_param(v),
   306	            Err(_) => log::warn!("invalid value for kernel parameter '{}'", name),
   307	        },
   308	        None => log::warn!("kernel parameter '{}' requires a value", name),
   309	    }
   310	}
   311	
   312	/// Helper for repeatable key-value parameters (all occurrences).
   313	pub fn setup_repeatable_kv_param<S: ParamStorage>(
   314	    storage: &S,
   315	    occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)],
   316	    name: &str,
   317	) where
   318	    S::Value: ParseRepeatableParamValue,
   319	{
   320	    let values: Vec<&str> = occurrences.iter().filter_map(|(_, val)| *val).collect();
   321	    if values.is_empty() {
   322	        log::warn!("repeatable parameter '{}' requires values", name);
   323	        return;
   324	    }
   325	    match S::Value::parse_all(&values) {
   326	        Ok(v) => storage.store_param(v),
   327	        Err(_) => log::warn!("invalid value for kernel parameter '{}'", name),
   328	    }
   329	}
   330	
   331	/// Helper for flag parameters (last-wins, optional value).
   332	pub fn setup_flag_param<S: ParamStorage>(
   333	    storage: &S,
   334	    occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)],
   335	    name: &str,
   336	) where
   337	    S::Value: ParseFlag,
   338	{
   339	    let last = match occurrences.last() {
   340	        Some((_, val)) => *val,
   341	        None => return,
   342	    };
   343	    match S::Value::parse_flag(last) {
   344	        Ok(v) => storage.store_param(v),
   345	        Err(_) => log::warn!("invalid value for flag '{}'", name),
   346	    }
   347	}

   349	pub static INIT_PROC_ARGS: Once<InitprocArgs> = Once::new();
   350	
   351	#[init_component]
   352	fn init() -> Result<(), ComponentInitError> {
   353	    INIT_PROC_ARGS.call_once(|| dispatch_params(ostd::boot::boot_info().kernel_cmdline.as_str()));
   354	
   355	    Ok(())
   356	}
   357	
   358	// Splits the command line string by spaces but preserve
   359	// ones that are protected by double quotes(`"`).
   360	fn split_arg(input: &str) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
   361	    let mut inside_quotes = false;
   362	
   363	    input.split(move |c: char| {
   364	        if c == '"' {
   365	            inside_quotes = !inside_quotes;
   366	        }
   367	
   368	        !inside_quotes && c.is_whitespace()
   369	    })
   370	}
   371	
   372	fn dispatch_params(cmdline: &str) -> InitprocArgs {
   373	    let mut result: InitprocArgs = InitprocArgs {
   374	        argv: Vec::new(),
   375	        envp: Vec::new(),
   376	    };
   377	
   378	    let mut kcmdline_end = false;
   379	
   380	    // Step 1: Tokenize and group by normalized parameter name.
   381	    let mut grouped: BTreeMap<String, Vec<(&str, Option<&str>)>> = BTreeMap::new();
   382	
   383	    for arg in split_arg(cmdline) {
   384	        // Everything after "--" goes to init.
   385	        if kcmdline_end {
   386	            result.argv.push(CString::new(arg).unwrap());
   387	            continue;
   388	        }
   389	        if arg == "--" {
   390	            kcmdline_end = true;
   391	            continue;
   392	        }
   393	
   394	        let (key, value) = match arg.find('=') {
   395	            Some(pos) => (&arg[..pos], Some(&arg[pos + 1..])),
   396	            None => (arg, None),
   397	        };
   398	        // Normalize hyphens to underscores (Linux compatibility)
   399	        let normalized = key.replace('-', "_");
   400	        // Group by normalized name, but keep the original key and value for forwarding.
   401	        grouped.entry(normalized).or_default().push((key, value));
   402	    }
   403	
   404	    // Step 2: Build lookup from registered param name to handler.
   405	    let registry: BTreeMap<&str, &KernelParam> = inventory::iter::<KernelParam>
   406	        .into_iter()
   407	        .map(|p| (p.name, p))
   408	        .collect();
   409	
   410	    // Step 3: Dispatch each group to its handler.
   411	    let mut recognized = Vec::new();
   412	    for (name, occurrences) in &grouped {
   413	        if let Some(param) = registry.get(name.as_str()) {
   414	            recognized.push((*param, occurrences));
   415	        } else {
   416	            // Unknown parameter: forward to init
   417	            if name.contains('.') {
   418	                // The entry contains a dot, which is treated as a module argument.
   419	                // Unrecognized module arguments are ignored.
   420	                continue;
   421	            } else if let Some((key, Some(value))) = occurrences.last().copied() {
   422	                // If the entry is not recognized, it is passed to the init process.
   423	                // Pattern 'entry=value' is treated as the init environment.
   424	                let envp_entry = CString::new(key.to_string() + "=" + value).unwrap();
   425	                result.envp.push(envp_entry);
   426	            } else if let Some((key, None)) = occurrences.last().copied() {
   427	                // If the entry is not recognized, it is passed to the init process.
   428	                // Pattern 'entry' without value is treated as the init argument.
   429	                let argv_entry = CString::new(key.to_string()).unwrap();
   430	                result.argv.push(argv_entry);
   431	            } else {
   432	                // This case should not happen since `occurrences` should never be empty.
   433	                debug_assert!(!occurrences.is_empty());
   434	            }
   435	        }
   436	    }
   437	
   438	    // Step 4: Call the setup function for each recognized parameter.
   439	    let (early_params, params): (Vec<_>, Vec<_>) =
   440	        recognized.into_iter().partition(|(p, _)| p.early);
   441	    early_params
   442	        .into_iter()
   443	        .chain(params)
   444	        .for_each(|(param, occurrences)| (param.setup)(occurrences));
   445	
   446	    result
   447	}
   448	
   449	macro_rules! define_unimplemented_param {
   450	    ($($name:expr),+ $(,)?) => {
   451	        $(
   452	            const _: () = {
   453	                fn __kparam_setup(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)]) {
   454	                    $crate::setup_unimplemented(occurrences, $name);
   455	                }
   456	                $crate::submit! {
   457	                    $crate::KernelParam { name: $name, setup: __kparam_setup, early: false }
   458	                }
   459	            };
   460	        )+
   461	    };
   462	}
   463	
   464	fn setup_unimplemented(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)], name: &str) {
   465	    if !occurrences.is_empty() {
   466	        log::warn!("kernel parameter '{}' is not yet implemented", name);
   467	    }
   468	}

   196	    fn send_command(&mut self, command: Command) -> Result<(), I8042ControllerError> {
   197	        if !self.read_status().contains(Status::INPUT_BUFFER_IS_FULL) {

   199	            Ok(())

   250	    /// before the acknowledgment is ignored.

   278	/// Timeout in milliseconds for sending commands or receiving data.
   279	///
   280	/// Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.17.9/source/drivers/input/serio/libps2.c#L344>
   281	#[repr(u16)]
   282	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
   283	enum Timeout {
   284	    /// Short timeout for normal commands (500 ms).
   285	    Short = 500,
   286	    /// Long timeout for the reset command (4000 ms).
   287	    Long = 4000,
   288	}
   289	
   290	/// Spins and waits until the timeout occurs or `f` returns `Some(_)`.
   291	//
   292	// TODO: The timeout is relatively large, up to several seconds. Therefore, spinning here is not
   293	// appropriate. The code needs to be refactored to use asynchronous interrupts.
   294	fn spin_wait_until<F, R>(timeout: Timeout, mut f: F) -> Option<R>
   295	where
   296	    F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
   297	{
   298	    use ostd::arch::{read_tsc, tsc_freq};
   299	
   300	    const MSEC_PER_SEC: u64 = 1000;
   301	
   302	    if let Some(res) = f() {
   303	        return Some(res);
   304	    }
   305	
   306	    let current = read_tsc();
   307	    let distance = tsc_freq() / MSEC_PER_SEC * (timeout as u16 as u64);
   308	    loop {
   309	        if let Some(res) = f() {
   310	            return Some(res);
   311	        }
   312	        if read_tsc().wrapping_sub(current) >= distance {
   313	            return None;
   314	        }
   315	        core::hint::spin_loop();
   316	    }
   317	}

   326	    OutputBusy,

   330	    DeviceAllocIrqFailed,

   333	/// The commands that can be sent to the PS/2 controller.
   334	///
   335	/// Reference: <https://wiki.osdev.org/I8042_PS/2_Controller#PS/2_Controller_Commands>.
   336	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
   337	#[repr(u8)]
   338	enum Command {
   339	    ReadConfiguration = 0x20,
   340	    WriteConfiguration = 0x60,
   341	    DisableSecondPort = 0xA7,
   342	    EnableSecondPort = 0xA8,
   343	    TestSecondPort = 0xA9,
   344	    TestController = 0xAA,
   345	    TestFirstPort = 0xAB,
   346	    DisableFirstPort = 0xAD,
   347	    EnableFirstPort = 0xAE,
   348	    WriteToSecondPort = 0xD4,
   349	}

   351	bitflags! {
   352	    /// The configuration of the PS/2 controller.
   353	    ///
   354	    /// Reference: <https://wiki.osdev.org/I8042_PS/2_Controller#PS/2_Controller_Configuration_Byte>.
   355	    struct Configuration: u8 {
   356	        /// First PS/2 port interrupt (1 = enabled, 0 = disabled)
   357	        const FIRST_PORT_INTERRUPT_ENABLED = 1 << 0;
   358	        /// Second PS/2 port interrupt (1 = enabled, 0 = disabled, only if 2 PS/2 ports supported)
   359	        const SECOND_PORT_INTERRUPT_ENABLED = 1 << 1;
   360	        /// System Flag (1 = system passed POST, 0 = your OS shouldn't be running)
   361	        const SYSTEM_POST_PASSED = 1 << 2;
   362	        /// First PS/2 port clock (1 = disabled, 0 = enabled)
   363	        const FIRST_PORT_CLOCK_DISABLED = 1 << 4;
   364	        /// Second PS/2 port clock (1 = disabled, 0 = enabled, only if 2 PS/2 ports supported)
   365	        const SECOND_PORT_CLOCK_DISABLED = 1 << 5;
   366	        /// First PS/2 port translation (1 = enabled, 0 = disabled)
   367	        const FIRST_PORT_TRANSLATION_ENABLED = 1 << 6;
   368	    }
   369	}
   370	
   371	bitflags! {
   372	    /// The status of the i8042 PS/2 controller.
   373	    ///
   374	    /// Reference: <https://wiki.osdev.org/I8042_PS/2_Controller#Status_Register>.
   375	    struct Status: u8 {
   376	        /// Output buffer status (0 = empty, 1 = full)
   377	        /// Must be set before attempting to read data from port 0x60.
   378	        const OUTPUT_BUFFER_IS_FULL = 1 << 0;
   379	        /// Input buffer status (0 = empty, 1 = full)
   380	        /// Must be clear before attempting to write data to IO port 0x60 or IO port 0x64.
   381	        const INPUT_BUFFER_IS_FULL = 1 << 1;
   382	        /// System Flag
   383	        /// Meant to be cleared on reset and set by firmware (via. PS/2 Controller Configuration Byte)
   384	        /// if the system passes self tests (POST).
   385	        const SYSTEM_FLAG = 1 << 2;
   386	        /// Command or data (0 = data, 1 = command)
   387	        /// Data written to input buffer is data for PS/2 device or command for controller.
   388	        const IS_COMMAND = 1 << 3;
   389	        /// Time-out error (0 = no error, 1 = time-out error)
   390	        const TIME_OUT_ERROR = 1 << 6;
   391	        /// Parity error (0 = no error, 1 = parity error)
   392	        const PARITY_ERROR = 1 << 7;
   393	    }
   394	}

   101	    pub fn singleton() -> &'static Arc<SystemConsole> {
   102	        static INSTANCE: Once<Arc<SystemConsole>> = Once::new();
   103	
   104	        INSTANCE.call_once(|| {
   105	            // TODO: Support specifying multiple TTY devices, e.g., "console=hvc0 console=tty0".
   106	            let console_name = CONSOLES
   107	                .get()
   108	                .and_then(|consoles| consoles.first().map(|s| s.as_str()))
   109	                .unwrap_or("tty0");
   110	
   111	            let device = match console_name {
   112	                "tty0" => Some(Arc::new(Tty0Device) as _),
   113	                "ttyS0" => serial0_device().cloned().map(|device| device as _),
   114	                "hvc0" => hvc0_device().cloned().map(|device| device as _),
   115	                _ => None,
   116	            };
   117	            let inner = device.unwrap_or_else(|| {
   118	                warn!(
   119	                    "'{}' console not found, falling back to 'tty0'",
   120	                    console_name
   121	                );
   122	                Arc::new(Tty0Device) as _
   123	            });
   124	
   125	            Arc::new(Self { inner })
   126	        })
   127	    }

    14	/// Prints the formatted arguments to the standard output.
    15	pub fn _print(args: fmt::Arguments) {
    16	    // We must call `all_devices_lock` instead of `all_devices` here, as `all_devices` invokes the
    17	    // `clone` method of `String` and `Arc`, which may lead to a deadlock when there is low memory
    18	    // in the heap. (The heap allocator will log a message when memory is low.)
    19	    //
    20	    // Also, holding the lock will prevent the logs from interleaving.
    21	    let devices = aster_console::all_devices_lock();
    22	
    23	    struct Printer<'a>(
    24	        SpinLockGuard<'a, BTreeMap<String, Arc<dyn AnyConsoleDevice>>, LocalIrqDisabled>,
    25	    );
    26	    impl Write for Printer<'_> {
    27	        fn write_str(&mut self, s: &str) -> fmt::Result {
    28	            if self.0.is_empty() {
    29	                ostd::early_print!("{}", s);
    30	            } else {
    31	                for console in self.0.values() {
    32	                    console.send(s.as_bytes());
    33	                }
    34	            }
    35	            Ok(())
    36	        }
    37	    }
    38	
    39	    Printer(devices).write_fmt(args).unwrap();
    40	}

    46	fn parse_kernel_commandline(boot_params: &BootParams) -> Option<&str> {
    47	    if boot_params.ext_cmd_line_ptr != 0 {
    48	        // TODO: We can support the above 4GiB command line after setting up
    49	        // linear mappings. By far, we cannot log the error because the serial is
    50	        // not up. Proceed as if there was no command line.
    51	        return None;
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    if boot_params.hdr.cmd_line_ptr == 0 || boot_params.hdr.cmdline_size == 0 {
    55	        return None;
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    let cmdline_ptr = paddr_to_vaddr(boot_params.hdr.cmd_line_ptr as usize);
    59	    let cmdline_len = boot_params.hdr.cmdline_size as usize;
    60	    // SAFETY: The command line is safe to read because of the contract with the loader.
    61	    let cmdline = unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(cmdline_ptr as *const u8, cmdline_len) };
    62	
    63	    // Now, unfortunately, there are silent errors because the serial is not up.
    64	    core::ffi::CStr::from_bytes_until_nul(cmdline)
    65	        .ok()?
    66	        .to_str()
    67	        .ok()
    68	}

    19

    21	    Some(unsafe { make_str_vaddr_static(name) })

    37	    let base_va = paddr_to_vaddr(start);
    38	    let length = end - start;
    39	    Some(unsafe { core::slice::from_raw_parts(base_va as *const u8, length) })
    40	}
    41	
    42	fn parse_acpi_arg() -> BootloaderAcpiArg {

codex
The normalized grouping does create a second correctness problem: unknown init arguments are no longer streamed in command-line order. Because the code iterates a `BTreeMap`, `init=/bin/sh zeta alpha` would hand `alpha` to `init` before `zeta`, and duplicates get collapsed to the last occurrence.
codex
I’m also validating the new `CpuList` parser against the Linux syntax it claims to implement. The grouped form is subtle, so I’m checking whether `a-b:x/y` means “every x CPUs” or “x CPUs per y-sized group.”

     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	//! Common parameter value types for the cmdline framework.
     4	//!
     5	//! This module provides Linux-style parsers that are frequently used by kernel
     6	//! command lines so users of this framework don't need to rewrite them.
     7	
     8	#![allow(dead_code)]
     9	
    10	extern crate alloc;
    11	
    12	use alloc::vec::Vec;
    13	
    14	use crate::{ParamError, ParseParamValue};
    15	
    16	/// Linux-style CPU list.
    17	///
    18	/// Examples:
    19	/// - `"1"`
    20	/// - `"1,2,10-20"`
    21	/// - `"100-2000:2/25"` (range with stride and optional group size)
    22	///
    23	/// The stored representation is a list of segments; expansion is optional.
    24	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    25	pub struct CpuList {
    26	    segments: Vec<CpuListSegment>,
    27	}
    28	
    29	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    30	pub struct CpuListSegment {
    31	    pub start: u32,
    32	    pub end: u32,
    33	    /// Step within range, default 1.
    34	    pub stride: u32,
    35	    /// Optional group size (the `/N` part). When present, stride selection is
    36	    /// applied within each group window.
    37	    pub group: Option<u32>,
    38	}
    39	
    40	/// Linux-style metric-suffixed u64 value.
    41	///
    42	/// Supports binary multiples (KiB-style):
    43	/// - `K` = 1024
    44	/// - `M` = 1024^2
    45	/// - `G` = 1024^3
    46	/// - `T` = 1024^4
    47	/// - `P` = 1024^5
    48	///
    49	/// Case-insensitive.
    50	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    51	pub struct MetricU64(pub u64);
    52	
    53	impl CpuList {
    54	    pub fn segments(&self) -> &[CpuListSegment] {
    55	        &self.segments
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    pub fn contains(&self, cpu: u32) -> bool {
    59	        self.segments.iter().any(|s| segment_contains(s, cpu))
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    /// Expand to concrete CPU IDs, returning at most `max_elems` elements.
    63	    ///
    64	    /// Note: if the CPU list expands to more than `max_elems`, the result is truncated.
    65	    pub fn expand_bounded(&self, max_elems: usize) -> Result<Vec<u32>, ParamError> {
    66	        if max_elems == 0 {
    67	            return Ok(Vec::new());
    68	        }
    69	
    70	        let mut out = Vec::new();
    71	
    72	        // Helper: push a cpu id; if we reached the bound, stop expansion.
    73	        let push_or_done = |out: &mut Vec<u32>, cpu: u32| -> bool {
    74	            if out.len() >= max_elems {
    75	                return true;
    76	            }
    77	            out.push(cpu);
    78	            out.len() >= max_elems
    79	        };
    80	
    81	        for s in &self.segments {
    82	            if s.stride == 0 {
    83	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
    84	            }
    85	            if let Some(g) = s.group
    86	                && g == 0
    87	            {
    88	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
    89	            }
    90	            if s.start > s.end {
    91	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
    92	            }
    93	
    94	            // Fast path: no group => arithmetic progression from start to end by stride.
    95	            if s.group.is_none() {
    96	                let mut cur = s.start;
    97	                while cur <= s.end {
    98	                    if push_or_done(&mut out, cur) {
    99	                        return Ok(out);
   100	                    }
   101	
   102	                    match cur.checked_add(s.stride) {
   103	                        Some(n) => cur = n,
   104	                        None => break,
   105	                    }
   106	                }
   107	                continue;
   108	            }
   109	
   110	            // Grouped selection:
   111	            // for each group window [start+gb, start+gb+g), emit start+gb+within for within=0,stride,2*stride...
   112	            let g = s.group.unwrap();
   113	            let stride = s.stride;
   114	
   115	            let mut gb: u32 = 0;
   116	            while let Some(v) = s.start.checked_add(gb) {
   117	                let group_start = v;
   118	                if group_start > s.end {
   119	                    break;
   120	                }
   121	
   122	                let mut within: u32 = 0;
   123	                while within < g {
   124	                    let cpu = match group_start.checked_add(within) {
   125	                        Some(v) => v,
   126	                        None => break,
   127	                    };
   128	                    if cpu > s.end {
   129	                        break;
   130	                    }
   131	
   132	                    if push_or_done(&mut out, cpu) {
   133	                        return Ok(out);
   134	                    }
   135	
   136	                    match within.checked_add(stride) {
   137	                        Some(n) => within = n,
   138	                        None => break,
   139	                    }
   140	                }
   141	
   142	                match gb.checked_add(g) {
   143	                    Some(n) => gb = n,
   144	                    None => break,
   145	                }
   146	            }
   147	        }
   148	
   149	        Ok(out)
   150	    }
   151	}
   152	
   153	fn segment_contains(s: &CpuListSegment, cpu: u32) -> bool {
   154	    if cpu < s.start || cpu > s.end {
   155	        return false;
   156	    }
   157	    if s.stride == 0 {
   158	        return false;
   159	    }
   160	
   161	    let offset = cpu - s.start;
   162	    if let Some(g) = s.group {
   163	        if g == 0 {
   164	            return false;
   165	        }
   166	        let in_group_offset = offset % g;
   167	        in_group_offset.is_multiple_of(s.stride)
   168	    } else {
   169	        offset.is_multiple_of(s.stride)
   170	    }
   171	}
   172	
   173	impl ParseParamValue for CpuList {
   174	    fn parse_param(value: &str) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   175	        if value.is_empty() {
   176	            return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   177	        }
   178	
   179	        let mut segments = Vec::new();
   180	        for part in value.split(',') {
   181	            if part.is_empty() {
   182	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   183	            }
   184	            segments.push(parse_cpu_segment(part)?);
   185	        }
   186	
   187	        Ok(CpuList { segments })
   188	    }
   189	}
   190	
   191	fn parse_cpu_segment(s: &str) -> Result<CpuListSegment, ParamError> {
   192	    // Grammar (pragmatic):
   193	    //   <range> [":" <stride> ["/" <group>] ]
   194	    // where <range> := <n> | <n> "-" <m>
   195	    let (range_part, tail) = match s.split_once(':') {
   196	        Some((a, b)) => (a, Some(b)),
   197	        None => (s, None),
   198	    };
   199	
   200	    let (start, end) = if let Some((a, b)) = range_part.split_once('-') {
   201	        (parse_u32(a)?, parse_u32(b)?)
   202	    } else {
   203	        let n = parse_u32(range_part)?;
   204	        (n, n)
   205	    };
   206	
   207	    if start > end {
   208	        return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   209	    }
   210	
   211	    let (stride, group) = match tail {
   212	        None => (1u32, None),
   213	        Some(t) => {
   214	            if t.is_empty() {
   215	                return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   216	            }
   217	            if let Some((stride_s, group_s)) = t.split_once('/') {
   218	                let stride = parse_u32(stride_s)?;
   219	                let group = parse_u32(group_s)?;
   220	                (stride, Some(group))
   221	            } else {
   222	                (parse_u32(t)?, None)
   223	            }
   224	        }
   225	    };
   226	
   227	    if stride == 0 {
   228	        return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   229	    }
   230	
   231	    if let Some(g) = group
   232	        && g == 0
   233	    {
   234	        return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   235	    }
   236	
   237	    Ok(CpuListSegment {
   238	        start,
   239	        end,
   240	        stride,
   241	        group,
   242	    })
   243	}
   244	
   245	fn parse_u32(s: &str) -> Result<u32, ParamError> {
   246	    if s.is_empty() {
   247	        return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   248	    }
   249	    s.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| ParamError::InvalidValue)
   250	}
   251	
   252	impl ParseParamValue for MetricU64 {
   253	    fn parse_param(value: &str) -> Result<Self, ParamError> {
   254	        if value.is_empty() {
   255	            return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   256	        }
   257	
   258	        let (num_part, suf) = match value.chars().last() {
   259	            Some(c) if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() => (&value[..value.len() - c.len_utf8()], Some(c)),
   260	            _ => (value, None),

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs | sed -n '260,460p'" in /root/asterinas/.agents/skills/aster-code-review/benchmark_runs/codex_all_20260727T115738Z/0100/scratch/work/wt1
 succeeded in 0ms:
   260	            _ => (value, None),
   261	        };
   262	
   263	        if num_part.is_empty() {
   264	            return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue);
   265	        }
   266	
   267	        let base: u64 = num_part.parse().map_err(|_| ParamError::InvalidValue)?;
   268	        let mul: u64 = match suf.map(|c| c.to_ascii_uppercase()) {
   269	            None => 1,
   270	            Some('K') => 1024u64,
   271	            Some('M') => 1024u64.pow(2),
   272	            Some('G') => 1024u64.pow(3),
   273	            Some('T') => 1024u64.pow(4),
   274	            Some('P') => 1024u64.pow(5),
   275	            _ => return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue),
   276	        };
   277	
   278	        base.checked_mul(mul)
   279	            .map(MetricU64)
   280	            .ok_or(ParamError::InvalidValue)
   281	    }
   282	}

codex
The CPU list grouped form is confirmed against Linux documentation: `100-2000:2/25` means “take two CPUs from each 25-wide group,” so the current stride-based interpretation would select `100,102,104,...` instead of `100,101,125,126,...`.

    27	/// Creates and schedules the init process to run.
    28	pub fn spawn_init_process(
    29	    executable_path: &str,
    30	    argv: Vec<CString>,
    31	    envp: Vec<CString>,
    32	) -> Result<Arc<Process>> {
    33	    // Ensure the path for init process executable is absolute.
    34	    debug_assert!(executable_path.starts_with('/'));
    35	
    36	    let process = create_init_process(executable_path, argv, envp)?;
    37	
    38	    set_session_and_group(&process);
    39	
    40	    process.run();
    41	
    42	    Ok(process)
    43	}
    44	
    45	fn create_init_process(
    46	    executable_path: &str,
    47	    argv: Vec<CString>,
    48	    envp: Vec<CString>,
    49	) -> Result<Arc<Process>> {
    50	    let fs = {
    51	        let fs_resolver = MountNamespace::get_init_singleton().new_path_resolver();
    52	        ThreadFsInfo::new(fs_resolver)
    53	    };
    54	    let fs_path = FsPath::try_from(executable_path)?;
    55	    let elf_path = fs.resolver().read().lookup(&fs_path)?;
    56	
    57	    let pid = allocate_posix_tid();
    58	    let vmar = Vmar::new(ProcessVm::new(elf_path.clone()));
    59	    let resource_limits = new_resource_limits_for_init();
    60	    let nice = Nice::default();
    61	    let oom_score_adj = 0;
    62	    let sig_dispositions = Arc::new(Mutex::new(SigDispositions::default()));
    63	    let user_ns = UserNamespace::get_init_singleton().clone();
    64	
    65	    let init_proc = Process::new(
    66	        pid,
    67	        vmar,
    68	        resource_limits,
    69	        nice,
    70	        oom_score_adj,
    71	        sig_dispositions,
    72	        user_ns,
    73	    );
    74	
    75	    let init_task = create_init_task(pid, &init_proc, fs, elf_path, argv, envp)?;
    76	    init_proc.tasks().lock().insert(init_task).unwrap();
    77	
    78	    Ok(init_proc)
    79	}
    80	
    81	fn set_session_and_group(process: &Arc<Process>) {
    82	    // Locking order: session table -> group table -> process table -> process group
    83	    let mut session_table_mut = process_table::session_table_mut();
    84	    let mut group_table_mut = process_table::group_table_mut();
    85	    let mut process_table_mut = process_table::process_table_mut();
    86	
    87	    // Create a new process group and session for the process
    88	    process.set_new_session(
    89	        &mut process.process_group.lock(),
    90	        &mut session_table_mut,
    91	        &mut group_table_mut,
    92	    );
    93	
    94	    // Add the new process to the global table
    95	    process_table_mut.insert(process.pid(), process.clone());
    96	}

    98	/// Creates the init task from the given executable file.
    99	fn create_init_task(
   100	    tid: Tid,
   101	    process: &Arc<Process>,
   102	    fs: ThreadFsInfo,
   103	    elf_path: Path,
   104	    argv: Vec<CString>,
   105	    envp: Vec<CString>,
   106	) -> Result<Arc<Task>> {
   107	    let credentials = Credentials::new_root();
   108	
   109	    let (elf_load_info, elf_abs_path) = {
   110	        let path_resolver = fs.resolver().read();
   111	
   112	        let program_to_load =
   113	            ProgramToLoad::build_from_file(elf_path.clone(), &path_resolver, argv, envp)?;
   114	        let vmar = process.lock_vmar();
   115	        let elf_load_info = program_to_load.load_to_vmar(vmar.unwrap(), &path_resolver)?;
   116	        let elf_abs_path = path_resolver.make_abs_path(&elf_path).into_string();
   117	
   118	        (elf_load_info, elf_abs_path)
   119	    };

    20	///
    21	/// This struct encapsulates the ELF file to be executed along with its header data,
    22	/// the `argv` and the `envp` which is required for the program execution.
    23	pub(super) struct ProgramToLoad {
    24	    elf_file: Path,
    25	    elf_headers: ElfHeaders,
    26	    argv: Vec<CString>,
    27	    envp: Vec<CString>,

    29	
    30	impl ProgramToLoad {
    31	    /// Constructs a new `ProgramToLoad` from a file and handles shebang interpretation if
    32	    /// necessary.
    33	    pub(super) fn build_from_file(
    34	        mut elf_file: Path,
    35	        path_resolver: &PathResolver,
    36	        mut argv: Vec<CString>,
    37	        envp: Vec<CString>,
    38	    ) -> Result<Self> {
    39	        check_executable_inode(elf_file.inode().as_ref())?;
    40	
    41	        // A limit to the recursion depth of shebang executables.
    42	        //
    43	        // If the interpreter is a shebang, then recursion will be triggered. If it loops, we
    44	        // should fail. We follow the same limit as Linux.
    45	        let mut recursive_limit = 5;
    46	
    47	        let (file_first_page, len) = loop {
    48	            // Read the first page of the file, which should contain a shebang or an ELF header.
    49	            let (file_first_page, len) = {
    50	                let mut buffer = Box::new([0u8; PAGE_SIZE]);
    51	                let len = elf_file.inode().read_bytes_at(0, &mut *buffer)?;
    52	                (buffer, len)
    53	            };
    54	
    55	            let Some(mut new_argv) = parse_shebang_line(&file_first_page[..len])? else {
    56	                break (file_first_page, len);
    57	            };
    58	
    59	            if recursive_limit == 0 {
    60	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ELOOP, "the recursieve limit is reached");
    61	            }
    62	            recursive_limit -= 1;
    63	
    64	            let interpreter = {
    65	                let filename = new_argv[0].to_str()?.to_string();
    66	                let fs_path = FsPath::try_from(filename.as_str())?;
    67	                path_resolver.lookup(&fs_path)?
    68	            };

    72	            new_argv.extend(argv);
    73	            argv = new_argv;
    74	            elf_file = interpreter;
    75	        };

    80	            elf_file,
    81	            elf_headers,
    82	            argv,
    83	            envp,
    84	        })

# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->

## Maintainability

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml` line 13

> ```diff
> [dependencies]
>  component.workspace = true
>  log.workspace = true
>  ostd.workspace = true
>  spin.workspace = true
> +inventory = { git = "https://github.com/asterinas/inventory", rev = "9dce587" }
> ```

`workspace-deps` (major): `inventory` is declared directly in `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml`, bypassing the workspace dependency table. That gives this crate its own dependency spelling/version source instead of the single workspace representation used for shared dependencies.

**Fix.** Move `inventory` to the root `[workspace.dependencies]` table, then reference it here as `inventory.workspace = true`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 179

> ```diff
> impl InitprocArgs {
>     /// Gets the argument vector (`argv`) of the init process.
>     pub fn argv(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
>         &self.argv
>     }
> 
>     /// Gets the environment vector (`envp`) of the init process.
>     pub fn envp(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
>         &self.envp
>     }
> }
> ```

`information-hiding` (minor): `InitprocArgs::argv` and `InitprocArgs::envp` expose `Vec` in their return types even though callers only need to inspect/copy the entries. This leaks the storage choice into the public API and makes a future representation change noisier.

**Fix.** Return slices from these getters, e.g. `pub fn argv(&self) -> &[CString]` and `pub fn envp(&self) -> &[CString]`; existing callers can still use `.to_vec()` on the slice.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 8

> ```diff
> //! command lines so users of this framework don't need to rewrite them.
> 
> #![allow(dead_code)]
> 
> extern crate alloc;
> ```

`narrow-lint-suppression` (minor): The module-level `#![allow(dead_code)]` suppresses `dead_code` for the entire `types` module, including any future private helpers added here. That makes unused code harder to notice and is broader than the actual planned-unused items.

**Fix.** Remove the module-level suppression. If a specific item is intentionally unused for a concrete future parameter, put `#[expect(dead_code)]` on that item with a short reason.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 30

> ```diff
> pub struct CpuListSegment {
>     pub start: u32,
>     pub end: u32,
>     /// Step within range, default 1.
>     pub stride: u32,
>     /// Optional group size (the `/N` part). When present, stride selection is
>     /// applied within each group window.
>     pub group: Option<u32>,
> }
> ```

`getter-encapsulation` (minor): `CpuListSegment` exposes all representation fields as `pub`, including invariant-bearing fields such as `stride` and `group`. Once consumers rely on these fields directly, it becomes harder to rename them or tighten invariants like non-zero stride/group values.

**Fix.** Keep the fields private and expose simple getters such as `start()`, `end()`, `stride()`, and `group()`. If external construction is needed, add a checked constructor instead of exposing the raw fields.

## Correctness

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 412

> 412:     for (name, occurrences) in &grouped {
> 413:         if let Some(param) = registry.get(name.as_str()) {

> 415:         } else {
> ...
> 421:             } else if let Some((key, Some(value))) = occurrences.last().copied() {
> 424:                 let envp_entry = CString::new(key.to_string() + "=" + value).unwrap();
> 425:                 result.envp.push(envp_entry);
> 426:             } else if let Some((key, None)) = occurrences.last().copied() {
> 429:                 let argv_entry = CString::new(key.to_string()).unwrap();
> 430:                 result.argv.push(argv_entry);
> ```

Incorrect argument forwarding (major): `dispatch_params()` forwards unknown init arguments by iterating `grouped`, a `BTreeMap`, after tokenization. That sorts arguments by key and collapses repeated occurrences to `occurrences.last()`: a command line such as `init=/bin/app zeta alpha zeta -- tail` reaches `init` as `tail alpha zeta` instead of preserving the command-line order and both `zeta` occurrences.

**Fix.** Do not derive forwarded `argv`/`envp` entries from the grouped `BTreeMap`. Record unknown non-module tokens during the original `split_arg()` scan, or make a second ordered pass over the original tokens after building `registry`, so every unknown occurrence is forwarded exactly once in command-line order.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 167

> ```diff
> 110:             // Grouped selection:
> 111:             // for each group window [start+gb, start+gb+g), emit start+gb+within for within=0,stride,2*stride...
> 112:             let g = s.group.unwrap();
> 113:             let stride = s.stride;
> ...
> 166:         let in_group_offset = offset % g;
> 167:         in_group_offset.is_multiple_of(s.stride)
> ```

Incorrect parser semantics (major): `CpuList` implements grouped ranges as a stride inside each group, but Linux’s documented `100-2000:2/25` syntax means “use `2` CPUs from the beginning of each `25`-CPU group” (`100,101,125,126,...`). This code instead treats `2` as a stride, so `contains(102)` returns `true` and `expand_bounded()` emits `100,102,104,...`.

**Fix.** Model the `:<used>/<group>` form as `used` plus `group`, not `stride` plus `group`. For grouped segments, `contains()` should check `offset % group < used`, and expansion should emit `group_start..group_start + used` clipped by `end`; keep stride behavior only for the non-grouped `:<stride>` form.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 275

> ```diff
> 268:         let mul: u64 = match suf.map(|c| c.to_ascii_uppercase()) {
> 269:             None => 1,
> 270:             Some('K') => 1024u64,
> 271:             Some('M') => 1024u64.pow(2),
> 272:             Some('G') => 1024u64.pow(3),
> 273:             Some('T') => 1024u64.pow(4),
> 274:             Some('P') => 1024u64.pow(5),
> 275:             _ => return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue),
> ```

Incorrect parser semantics (minor): `MetricU64` is introduced as a Linux-style metric suffix parser, but it rejects the valid Linux `E` suffix. `MetricU64::parse_param("1E")` currently returns `ParamError::InvalidValue` even though Linux metric suffixes include `E = 2^60`.

**Fix.** Add `Some('E') => 1024u64.pow(6)` and keep the existing `checked_mul()` overflow handling, then change the test that currently expects `MetricU64::parse_param("1E")` to fail.

### `kernel/comps/logger/src/console.rs` line 29

> ```diff
> 27:         fn write_str(&mut self, s: &str) -> fmt::Result {
> 28:             if self.0.is_empty() {
> 29:                 ostd::early_print!("{}", s);
> 30:             } else {
> 31:                 for console in self.0.values() {
> 32:                     console.send(s.as_bytes());
> ```

`no-io-under-spinlock` (major): `_print()` calls `ostd::early_print!` while still holding the `aster_console::all_devices_lock()` spinlock. `ostd::early_print!` locks `SERIAL_PORT` and writes to the serial device, so the fallback path now performs device I/O under the console-device spinlock.

**Fix.** Release the `all_devices_lock()` guard before using the early serial fallback. For example, check `devices.is_empty()` outside the formatter path, `drop(devices)`, then call `ostd::early_print!`; keep the existing locked path only for registered console devices.

## Documentation

### `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` line 24

> ```diff
> +This parameter may be specified multiple times.
> +Kernel messages are delivered to each listed console.
> ...
> +            let console_name = CONSOLES
> +                .get()
> +                .and_then(|consoles| consoles.first().map(|s| s.as_str()))
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): The `console` documentation says kernel messages are delivered to each listed console, but `SystemConsole::singleton()` still takes only `CONSOLES.first()`. A user booting with `console=ttyS0 console=hvc0` will not get both consoles despite this page saying they should.

**Fix.** Update the `console` entry to document the current first-console-only behavior, or implement multi-console delivery before claiming that every listed `console` receives messages.

### `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` line 62

> ```diff
> +- `0`, `off`, `no`, `false` - treat the i8042 controller as absent (skip probing)
> ...
> +        if !Self::is_present_acpi() {
> +            if !Self::is_present_cmdline() {
> +                return Err(I8042ControllerError::NotPresent);
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): The `i8042.exist=0` documentation says it treats the controller as absent and skips probing, but the code only consults `I8042_EXIST` when ACPI already says the controller is absent. If ACPI says present, `i8042.exist=0` does not skip probing.

**Fix.** Document `i8042.exist` as a positive override only, and either remove the false-valued examples or state that `0`, `off`, `no`, and `false` merely disable the positive override and do not force an ACPI-present controller absent.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 473

> ```diff
> +define_unimplemented_param!(
> +    "tsc",
> +    "no_timer_check",
> +    "reboot",
> +    "pci",
> +    "debug",
> +    "panic",
> +    "nr_cpus",

> +    "initrd"

> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): This change registers Linux kernel parameters such as `tsc`, `no_timer_check`, `reboot`, `pci`, `debug`, `panic`, `nr_cpus`, `selinux`, and `initrd` as recognized and consumed, but `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` does not document that user-visible behavior. Users cannot tell these names are swallowed with a warning instead of being forwarded to `init`.

**Fix.** Add these recognized-but-unimplemented parameters to the Kernel Parameters compatibility page, preferably in an `Recognized but unsupported` table that states they are consumed and warn without implementing Linux behavior.

// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

//! The module to parse kernel command-line arguments.
//!
//! The format of the Asterinas command line string conforms
//! to the Linux kernel command line rules:
//!
//! <https://www.kernel.org/doc/html/v6.4/admin-guide/kernel-parameters.html>
//!
#![no_std]
#![deny(unsafe_code)]

extern crate alloc;

use alloc::{
    collections::BTreeMap,
    ffi::CString,
    string::{String, ToString},
    vec,
    vec::Vec,
};

use component::{ComponentInitError, init_component};
use spin::Once;

#[derive(PartialEq, Debug)]
struct InitprocArgs {
    path: Option<String>,
    argv: Vec<CString>,
    envp: Vec<CString>,
}

/// Kernel module arguments
#[derive(PartialEq, Debug, Clone)]
pub enum ModuleArg {
    /// A string argument
    Arg(CString),
    /// A key-value argument
    KeyVal(CString, CString),
}

/// The struct to store the parsed kernel command-line arguments.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct KCmdlineArg {
    initproc: InitprocArgs,
    console_names: Vec<String>,
    module_args: BTreeMap<String, Vec<ModuleArg>>,
}

// Define get APIs.
impl KCmdlineArg {
    /// Gets the path of the init process.
    pub fn get_initproc_path(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        self.initproc.path.as_deref()
    }

    /// Gets the argument vector (`argv`) of the init process.
    pub fn get_initproc_argv(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
        &self.initproc.argv
    }

    /// Gets the environment vector (`envp`) of the init process.
    pub fn get_initproc_envp(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
        &self.initproc.envp
    }

    /// Gets the vector of system console names specified in the command line.
    pub fn get_console_names(&self) -> &Vec<String> {
        &self.console_names
    }

    /// Gets the argument vector of a kernel module.
    pub fn get_module_args(&self, module: &str) -> Option<&Vec<ModuleArg>> {
        self.module_args.get(module)
    }
}

// Splits the command line string by spaces but preserve
// ones that are protected by double quotes(`"`).
fn split_arg(input: &str) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
    let mut inside_quotes = false;

    input.split(move |c: char| {
        if c == '"' {
            inside_quotes = !inside_quotes;
        }

        !inside_quotes && c.is_whitespace()
    })
}

// Define the way to parse a string to `KCmdlineArg`.
impl From<&str> for KCmdlineArg {
    fn from(cmdline: &str) -> Self {
        // What we construct.
        let mut result: KCmdlineArg = KCmdlineArg {
            initproc: InitprocArgs {
                path: None,
                argv: Vec::new(),
                envp: Vec::new(),
            },
            console_names: Vec::new(),
            module_args: BTreeMap::new(),
        };

        // Every thing after the "--" mark is the init arguments.
        let mut kcmdline_end = false;

        // The main parse loop. The processing steps are arranged (not very strictly)
        // by the analysis over the Backus–Naur form syntax tree.
        for arg in split_arg(cmdline) {
            // Cmdline => KernelArg "--" InitArg
            // KernelArg => Arg "\s+" KernelArg | %empty
            // InitArg => Arg "\s+" InitArg | %empty
            if kcmdline_end {
                if result.initproc.path.is_none() {
                    panic!("[KCmdline] Init arguments specified without init process");
                }
                result.initproc.argv.push(CString::new(arg).unwrap());
                continue;
            }
            if arg == "--" {
                kcmdline_end = true;
                continue;
            }
            // Arg => Entry | Entry "=" Value
            let arg_pattern: Vec<_> = arg.split('=').collect();
            let (entry, value) = match arg_pattern.len() {
                1 => (arg_pattern[0], None),
                2 => (arg_pattern[0], Some(arg_pattern[1])),
                _ => {
                    log::warn!(
                        "[KCmdline] Unable to parse kernel argument {}, skip for now",
                        arg
                    );
                    continue;
                }
            };
            // Entry => Module "." ModuleOptionName | KernelOptionName
            let entry_pattern: Vec<_> = entry.split('.').collect();
            let (node, option) = match entry_pattern.len() {
                1 => (None, entry_pattern[0]),
                2 => (Some(entry_pattern[0]), entry_pattern[1]),
                _ => {
                    log::warn!(
                        "[KCmdline] Unable to parse entry {} in argument {}, skip for now",
                        entry,
                        arg
                    );
                    continue;
                }
            };
            if let Some(modname) = node {
                let modarg = if let Some(v) = value {
                    ModuleArg::KeyVal(
                        CString::new(option.to_string()).unwrap(),
                        CString::new(v).unwrap(),
                    )
                } else {
                    ModuleArg::Arg(CString::new(option).unwrap())
                };
                result
                    .module_args
                    .entry(modname.to_string())
                    .and_modify(|v| v.push(modarg.clone()))
                    .or_insert(vec![modarg.clone()]);
                continue;
            }
            // KernelOptionName => /*literal string alternatives*/ | /*init environment*/
            if let Some(value) = value {
                // The option has a value.
                match option {
                    "init" => {
                        if result.initproc.path.is_some() {
                            panic!("[KCmdline] Init process specified twice");
                        }
                        result.initproc.path = Some(value.to_string());
                    }
                    "console" => {
                        result.console_names.push(value.to_string());

codex
The workspace dependency and API-shape comments are confirmed against the repo’s root dependency table and public getters. I’m also checking whether any comments share the same root cause before filling the summary.

/bin/bash -lc "git show b205cacbf5e5406172e5c7216179c2559c091885:kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs | sed -n '180,320p'" in /root/asterinas/.agents/skills/aster-code-review/benchmark_runs/codex_all_20260727T115738Z/0100/scratch/work/wt1
 succeeded in 0ms:
                        result.console_names.push(value.to_string());
                    }
                    _ => {
                        // If the option is not recognized, it is passed to the init process.
                        // Pattern 'option=value' is treated as the init environment.
                        let envp_entry = CString::new(option.to_string() + "=" + value).unwrap();
                        result.initproc.envp.push(envp_entry);
                    }
                }
            } else {
                // There is no value, the entry is only a option.

                // If the option is not recognized, it is passed to the init process.
                // Pattern 'option' without value is treated as the init argument.
                let argv_entry = CString::new(option.to_string()).unwrap();
                result.initproc.argv.push(argv_entry);
            }
        }

        result
    }
}

/// The [`KCmdlineArg`] singleton.
pub static KCMDLINE: Once<KCmdlineArg> = Once::new();

#[init_component]
fn init() -> Result<(), ComponentInitError> {
    KCMDLINE.call_once(|| KCmdlineArg::from(ostd::boot::boot_info().kernel_cmdline.as_str()));

    Ok(())
}

# Summary

This series cleanly moves kernel parameters toward a declarative registration model and adds useful typed parsers/tests for common Linux-style values. The main risk is that the new dispatcher groups parameters before deciding what to forward, which changes observable init argument ordering and drops duplicate unknown arguments.

Top issues:

- Major: `dispatch_params()` forwards unknown init arguments from a sorted `BTreeMap`, so pre-`--` arguments are reordered and duplicate unknown entries collapse to the last occurrence.
- Major: grouped `CpuList` ranges implement `N-M:used/group` as a stride, not Linux's documented "used CPUs per group" semantics.
- Major: the logger fallback performs early serial output while holding the console-device spinlock.
- Major: the new kernel-parameter compatibility docs overstate implemented behavior for `console`, `i8042.exist=0`, and recognized-but-unimplemented Linux parameters.

Structurally, the parameter framework would be easier to evolve if shared dependencies stayed in the workspace table and public getters avoided exposing `Vec` representation details.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml` line 13

> ```diff
> [dependencies]
>  component.workspace = true
>  log.workspace = true
>  ostd.workspace = true
>  spin.workspace = true
> +inventory = { git = "https://github.com/asterinas/inventory", rev = "9dce587" }
> ```

`workspace-deps` (major): `inventory` is declared directly in `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml`, bypassing the workspace dependency table. That gives this crate its own dependency spelling/version source instead of the single workspace representation used for shared dependencies.

**Fix.** Move `inventory` to the root `[workspace.dependencies]` table, then reference it here as `inventory.workspace = true`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 179

> ```diff
> impl InitprocArgs {
>     /// Gets the argument vector (`argv`) of the init process.
>     pub fn argv(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
>         &self.argv
>     }
> 
>     /// Gets the environment vector (`envp`) of the init process.
>     pub fn envp(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
>         &self.envp
>     }
> }
> ```

`information-hiding` (minor): `InitprocArgs::argv` and `InitprocArgs::envp` expose `Vec` in their return types even though callers only need to inspect/copy the entries. This leaks the storage choice into the public API and makes a future representation change noisier.

**Fix.** Return slices from these getters, e.g. `pub fn argv(&self) -> &[CString]` and `pub fn envp(&self) -> &[CString]`; existing callers can still use `.to_vec()` on the slice.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 8

> ```diff
> //! command lines so users of this framework don't need to rewrite them.
> 
> #![allow(dead_code)]
> 
> extern crate alloc;
> ```

`narrow-lint-suppression` (minor): The module-level `#![allow(dead_code)]` suppresses `dead_code` for the entire `types` module, including any future private helpers added here. That makes unused code harder to notice and is broader than the actual planned-unused items.

**Fix.** Remove the module-level suppression. If a specific item is intentionally unused for a concrete future parameter, put `#[expect(dead_code)]` on that item with a short reason.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 30

> ```diff
> pub struct CpuListSegment {
>     pub start: u32,
>     pub end: u32,
>     /// Step within range, default 1.
>     pub stride: u32,

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+This series cleanly moves kernel parameters toward a declarative registration model and adds useful typed parsers/tests for common Linux-style values. The main risk is that the new dispatcher groups parameters before deciding what to forward, which changes observable init argument ordering and drops duplicate unknown arguments.
+
+Top issues:
+
+- Major: `dispatch_params()` forwards unknown init arguments from a sorted `BTreeMap`, so pre-`--` arguments are reordered and duplicate unknown entries collapse to the last occurrence.
+- Major: grouped `CpuList` ranges implement `N-M:used/group` as a stride, not Linux's documented "used CPUs per group" semantics.
